   141	
   142	// ── The SUBJECT of a build ──────────────────────────────────────────────────
   143	// The subject is the code under review. It is NOT everything the branch
   144	// touched, because a branch also carries the evidence ABOUT the change:
   145	// review records, raw reviewer transcripts, the task packet, the ledger.
   146	//
   147	// Those must be excluded for two independent reasons:
   148	//
   149	//   1. A review cannot be part of the hash it certifies. If review records were
   150	//      in the subject diff, writing one would change the hash, which would
   151	//      invalidate the record that was just written — no set of reviews could
   152	//      ever satisfy the gate. Excluding them makes adding evidence a no-op on
   153	//      the subject hash, which is what lets the loop terminate at all.
   154	//
   155	//   2. Control metadata must not escalate risk. builder-control/ is high-risk
   156	//      because the control CODE is high-risk. A perfectly ordinary front-end PR
   157	//      that happens to carry its own packet and review records would otherwise
   158	//      inherit "the control system itself" and be forced to HIGH forever.
   159	//
   160	// Control CODE (gate.cjs, engineering-os.cjs, the doctrine) stays in the
   161	// subject and stays high-risk. Only evidence artifacts are excluded.
   162	const EVIDENCE_EXCLUDE = [
   163	  'builder-control/reviews/**',
   164	  'builder-control/review-raw/**',
   165	  'builder-control/packets/**',
   166	  'builder-control/ledger.json',
   167	];
   168	
   169	function isEvidencePath(p) {
   170	  return EVIDENCE_EXCLUDE.some((g) => globToRe(g).test(p));
   171	}
   172	
   173	// Compute subject paths + the hash reviewers bind to.
   174	// changedPaths may be supplied explicitly; otherwise it is derived from git.
   175	function computeSubject({ base, head, changed }) {
   176	  let changedPaths;
   177	  if (Array.isArray(changed) && changed.length) {
   178	    changedPaths = changed.slice();
   179	  } else {
   180	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   181	    if (base) {
   182	      const b = assertRef(base, '--base');
   183	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', `${b}..${h}`]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   184	    } else {
   185	      changedPaths = git(['diff', '--name-only', h]).split('\n').filter(Boolean);
   186	    }
   187	  }
   188	  changedPaths = [...new Set(changedPaths)].sort();
   189	
   190	  const subjectPaths = changedPaths.filter((p) => !isEvidencePath(p));
   191	  const excluded = changedPaths.filter(isEvidencePath);
   192	
   193	  // Hash ONLY the subject paths. `--` separates paths from refs so a path that
   194	  // looks like a ref cannot be reinterpreted as one.
   195	  let diff = '';
   196	  if (subjectPaths.length) {
   197	    const h = assertRef(head || 'HEAD', '--head');
   198	    const args = ['diff'];
   199	    if (base) args.push(`${assertRef(base, '--base')}..${h}`);
   200	    else args.push(h);
   201	    args.push('--', ...subjectPaths);
   202	    diff = git(args);
   203	  }
   204	
   205	  return {
   206	    changedPaths,
   207	    subjectPaths,
   208	    excludedAsEvidence: excluded,
   209	    subjectSha256: sha256(diff),
   210	    diffBytes: diff.length,
   211	    range: base ? `${base}..${head || 'HEAD'}` : (head || 'HEAD'),
   212	  };
   213	}
   214	
   215	function parseArgs(argv) {
   216	  const a = { changed: [], review: [], _: [] };
   217	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   218	    const t = argv[i];
   219	    if (t === '--changed') a.changed.push(argv[++i]);
   220	    else if (t === '--review') a.review.push(argv[++i]);
   640	  let bad = 0;
   641	  const results = files.map(loadReview);
   642	  if (args.json) {
   643	    out(JSON.stringify(results.map((r) => ({ path: r.path, ok: r.ok, errors: r.errors })), null, 2));
   644	    return results.every((r) => r.ok) ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_USAGE;
   645	  }
   646	  out('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEW RECORD VALIDATION');
   647	  out('='.repeat(52));
   648	  for (const r of results) {
   649	    if (r.ok) { out(`ok    ${r.path}  (${r.rec.reviewer} / ${r.rec.disposition})`); }
   650	    else { bad++; out(`FAIL  ${r.path}`); for (const e of r.errors) out(`        ${e}`); }
   651	  }
   652	  out('');
   653	  out(bad ? `${bad} invalid record(s).` : `All ${results.length} record(s) valid.`);
   654	  return bad ? EXIT_USAGE : EXIT_PASS;
   655	}
   656	
   657	// Gather review records from explicit --review args and from the conventional
   658	// evidence directory, so CI does not have to enumerate them.
   659	function collectReviewPaths(args) {
   660	  const explicit = args.review.slice();
   661	  const dir = path.join(HERE, 'reviews');
   662	  const fromDir = fs.existsSync(dir)
   663	    ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json')).map((f) => path.join(dir, f))
   664	    : [];
   665	  return [...new Set([...explicit, ...fromDir])];
   666	}
   667	
   668	// ── COMMAND: --gate-done ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   669	// The fail-closed question: given what actually exists on disk, has this change
   670	// earned the right to move forward?
   671	//
   672	// Terminal states are deliberately NOT "DONE". Reviews existing is not the same
   673	// as software being correct, and a word that implies finished invites everyone
   674	// downstream to stop checking.
   675	const STATE_READY_DET = 'READY_FOR_DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION';
   676	const STATE_READY_PR = 'READY_FOR_PR';
   677	
   678	function gateDone(args) {
   679	  const problems = [];
   680	  const observed = [];
   681	
   682	  // 1. Subject binding is MANDATORY and always enforced. Without it there is
   683	  //    no answer to "reviewed WHAT?", and every downstream check is decoration.
   684	  const subject = computeSubject({ base: args.base, head: args.head, changed: args.changed });
   685	  const claimed = args.subjectSha || args.diffSha || null;
   686	  if (!claimed) {
   687	    problems.push({
   688	      rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING',
   689	      detail: 'no --subject-sha was supplied. A gate decision with no bound subject certifies nothing. Compute it with `--subject --json` and pass it back.',
   690	    });
   691	  } else if (!/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/.test(claimed)) {
   692	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-NO-SUBJECT-BINDING', detail: `--subject-sha ${JSON.stringify(claimed)} is not a sha256 hex digest` });
   693	  } else if (claimed !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   694	    problems.push({
   695	      rule: 'ENGOS-SUBJECT-MISMATCH',
   696	      detail: `--subject-sha ${claimed.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the subject computed here (${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…). The tree moved, or the wrong subject was supplied.`,
   697	    });
   698	  } else {
   699	    observed.push(`subject bound: ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 16)}… over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} path(s)`);
   700	  }
   701	  if (subject.excludedAsEvidence.length) {
   702	    observed.push(`${subject.excludedAsEvidence.length} evidence path(s) excluded from the subject — adding review records does not move this hash`);
   703	  }
   704	
   705	  // 2. Lane, computed from the SUBJECT only.
   706	  const cls = classify(subject.subjectPaths, { milestone: args.milestone, novel: args.novel, diffLines: args.diffLines });
   707	  observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? ' (high-risk)' : ''}; required reviewers: ${cls.requiredReviewers.join(', ') || 'none'}`);
   708	
   709	  // 3. Packet: required for every lane except LIGHT.
   710	  let packetId = null;
   711	  if (cls.requiresPacket) {
   712	    if (!args.packet) {
   713	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `lane ${cls.lane} requires a task packet; none was supplied` });
   714	    } else if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) {
   715	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-REQUIRED', detail: `packet not found: ${args.packet}` });
   716	    } else {
   717	      const pv = spawnSync('node', [PACKET_TOOLS, '--validate', args.packet], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' });
   718	      if (pv.status !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-PACKET-INVALID', detail: `packet-tools rejected the packet (exit ${pv.status}). Fix the packet first.` });
   719	      else observed.push('task packet is valid (packet-tools.cjs)');
   720	      try { packetId = readJSON(args.packet).packetId; } catch { /* reported above */ }
   721	
   722	      const sp = specCheck(args.packet);
   723	      if (!sp.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED', detail: `${sp.unresolved.length} source(s) of truth are neither pinnable nor marked UNVERIFIED: ${sp.unresolved.map((r) => r.source).join(', ')}` });
   724	      else observed.push(`spec pin: ${sp.rows.length - sp.unverified.length} pinned, ${sp.unverified.length} explicitly UNVERIFIED`);
   725	      var specRows = sp;
   726	    }
   727	  } else {
   728	    observed.push(`lane ${cls.lane}: no task packet required — deterministic checks are the whole gate here`);
   729	  }
   730	
   731	  // 4. Load records. Malformed records are errors; they are never skipped.
   732	  const loaded = collectReviewPaths(args).map(loadReview);
   733	  for (const l of loaded) if (!l.ok) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MALFORMED', detail: `${l.path}: ${l.errors.join('; ')}` });
   734	  const allValid = loaded.filter((l) => l.ok).map((l) => l.rec);
   735	
   736	  // 5. Partition by subject hash BEFORE anything else looks at findings.
   737	  //    A record about a different subject is not "stale evidence to weigh" —
   738	  //    it is evidence about a different thing, and it must not contaminate
   739	  //    this decision in either direction. It cannot approve this change and
   740	  //    its findings cannot block it.
   741	  const bound = [];
   742	  const foreign = [];
   743	  for (const rec of allValid) {
   744	    const sha = rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.diffSha256;
   745	    if (sha === subject.subjectSha256) bound.push(rec);
   746	    else foreign.push(rec);
   747	  }
   748	  if (foreign.length) {
   749	    observed.push(`${foreign.length} review record(s) bound to a different subject were IGNORED (they neither approve nor block this change)`);
   750	  }
   751	
   752	  // 6. Supersession — the one documented deterministic replacement rule.
   753	  //    A record may declare `supersedes: <reviewId>`; the named record is
   754	  //    dropped. Anything else that leaves two records for the same reviewer on
   755	  //    the same subject is AMBIGUOUS and blocks, because picking a winner by
   756	  //    timestamp or file order would let whichever record happened to sort
   757	  //    later silently overrule a rejection.
   758	  const superseded = new Set();
   759	  for (const rec of bound) if (rec.supersedes) superseded.add(rec.supersedes);
   760	  const active = bound.filter((r) => !superseded.has(r.reviewId));
   761	  if (superseded.size) observed.push(`${superseded.size} record(s) superseded by an explicit supersedes declaration`);
   762	
   763	  const byReviewer = {};
   764	  for (const rec of active) (byReviewer[rec.reviewer] ||= []).push(rec);
   765	  for (const [who, recs] of Object.entries(byReviewer)) {
   766	    if (recs.length > 1) {
   767	      problems.push({
   768	        rule: 'ENGOS-AMBIGUOUS-REVIEWS',
   769	        detail: `${recs.length} active records from "${who}" for this subject (${recs.map((r) => `${r.reviewId}:${r.disposition}`).join(', ')}). Exactly one verdict per reviewer per subject. Resolve by adding "supersedes" to the record that replaces the other.`,
   770	      });
   771	    }
   772	  }
   773	
   774	  // 7. Packet binding of records.
   775	  if (packetId) {
   776	    for (const rec of active) {
   777	      if (rec.packetId !== packetId) {
   778	        problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-WRONG-PACKET', detail: `${rec.reviewId} is bound to packet ${rec.packetId}, not ${packetId}` });
   779	      }
   780	    }
   781	  }
   782	
   783	  // 8. Coverage: a record must have read the WHOLE subject. Subset coverage is
   784	  //    the quiet failure — a reviewer that saw 3 of 11 files reporting APPROVE
   785	  //    looks identical to one that saw everything.
   786	  const subjectSet = new Set(subject.subjectPaths);
   787	  for (const rec of active) {
   788	    if (rec.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE') continue;
   789	    const seen = new Set((rec.reviewOf && rec.reviewOf.changedPaths) || []);
   790	    const missing = [...subjectSet].filter((p) => !seen.has(p));
   791	    const extra = [...seen].filter((p) => !subjectSet.has(p));
   792	    if (missing.length) {
   793	      problems.push({
   794	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-SHORT',
   795	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) did not cover ${missing.length} subject path(s): ${missing.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}${missing.length > 5 ? ' …' : ''}. Partial coverage is not approval of the whole change.`,
   796	      });
   797	    }
   798	    if (extra.length) {
   799	      problems.push({
   800	        rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-COVERAGE-EXTRA',
   801	        detail: `${rec.reviewId} (${rec.reviewer}) claims to have reviewed ${extra.length} path(s) that are not in the subject: ${extra.slice(0, 5).join(', ')}. The record does not describe this change.`,
   802	      });
   803	    }
   804	  }
   805	
   806	  // 9. Every required reviewer must have an APPROVE on this exact subject.
   807	  if (byReviewer['claude-self']) {
   808	    observed.push('claude-self record present (recorded for traceability; it satisfies no required slot)');
   809	  }
   810	  for (const need of cls.requiredReviewers) {
   811	    const recs = byReviewer[need] || [];
   812	    if (!recs.length) {
   813	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING', detail: `${need} is required for lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? '/high-risk' : ''} and has no valid review record bound to this subject. Missing evidence is a BLOCK, never an implicit pass.` });
   814	      continue;
   815	    }
   816	    const unavailable = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   817	    if (unavailable) {
   818	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${need} reported UNAVAILABLE: ${unavailable.unavailableReason}. Reported honestly — and it still blocks, because this lane requires that review.` });
   819	      continue;
   820	    }
   821	    const rejected = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT');
   822	    if (rejected) { problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED', detail: `${need} returned REJECT (${rejected.reviewId})` }); continue; }
   823	    observed.push(`${need}: ${recs.map((r) => r.disposition).join(', ')}`);
   824	  }
   825	
   826	  // 10. Blocking findings — from ACTIVE, SUBJECT-BOUND records only.
   827	  const blocking = [];
   828	  for (const rec of active) {
   829	    for (const f of rec.findings || []) {
   830	      if ((f.severity === 'CRITICAL' || f.severity === 'HIGH') && f.status === 'OPEN') {
   831	        blocking.push({ reviewer: rec.reviewer, severity: f.severity, file: f.file || '(unspecified)', problem: f.problem });
   832	      }
   833	      // A FIXED finding must name a re-verifying record that is itself active
   834	      // and bound to this subject, and that reviewer may not be the builder.
   835	      if (f.status === 'FIXED') {
   836	        const v = active.find((r) => r.reviewId === f.verifiedByReviewId);
   837	        if (!v) {
   838	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-UNVERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED cites verifiedByReviewId "${f.verifiedByReviewId}", which is not an active review record for this subject.` });
   839	        } else if (v.reviewer === 'claude-self') {
   840	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-SELF-VERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED is verified only by claude-self (${v.reviewId}). The builder cannot verify its own fix.` });
   841	        }
   842	      }
   843	    }
   844	  }
   845	  for (const b of blocking) {
   846	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-OPEN-BLOCKING-FINDING', detail: `${b.severity} from ${b.reviewer} in ${b.file}: ${b.problem}` });
   847	  }
   848	
   849	  // 11. Unverified items surfaced, never dropped.
   850	  const unverified = [];
   851	  for (const rec of active) for (const u of rec.unverified || []) unverified.push(`${rec.reviewer}: ${u}`);
   852	  if (typeof specRows !== 'undefined' && specRows) for (const r of specRows.unverified) unverified.push(`spec: ${r.source}`);
   853	
   854	  // 12. Deterministic checks. READY_FOR_PR is only reachable when this process
   855	  //     actually executed them — not when a caller asserts they passed.
   856	  let checks = null;
   857	  if (args.runChecks) {
   858	    if (!args.packet) {
   859	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-CHECKS-NO-PACKET', detail: '--run-checks needs a packet to read testsRequired from' });
   860	    } else {
   861	      checks = runDeterministicChecks(args.packet);
   862	      for (const c of checks) if (c.exit !== 0) problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED', detail: `${c.cmd} exited ${c.exit}` });
   863	      observed.push(`deterministic checks executed here: ${checks.filter((c) => c.exit === 0).length}/${checks.length} passed`);
   864	    }
   865	  }
   866	
   867	  const ok = problems.length === 0;
   868	  const state = !ok ? 'BLOCKED' : (checks ? STATE_READY_PR : STATE_READY_DET);
   869	
   870	  return { ok, state, problems, observed, unverified, classification: cls, subject, reviewsBound: bound.length, reviewsActive: active.length, reviewsForeign: foreign.length, checks };

    68	// ── policy-based routing (MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json) ────────────────────────
    69	const POLICY_PATH = require('path').join(__dirname, 'MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json');
    70	
    71	function loadPolicy() {
    72	  const fs = require('fs');
    73	  if (!fs.existsSync(POLICY_PATH)) {
    74	    const e = new Error('MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json is missing. Refusing to route: an absent policy is not a permissive policy.');
    75	    e.refused = true; throw e;
    76	  }
    77	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(POLICY_PATH, 'utf8'));
    78	}
    79	
    80	// Metered execution needs a named human and a cap inside the hard ceiling.
    81	// Each condition exists because of a specific way spending goes wrong:
    82	// no name = nobody accountable; no cap = unbounded; cap above ceiling = the
    83	// ceiling was decorative.
    84	function meteredAuthorization(opts) {
    85	  let policy;
    86	  try { policy = loadPolicy(); }
    87	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: 'model routing policy unreadable — metered execution refused' }; }
    88	  const b = policy.budgets || {};
    89	  if (!opts.allowMetered) return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution has no explicit budget authorization' };
    90	  if (!opts.approvedBy || String(opts.approvedBy).trim() === '') {
    91	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --approved-by <named human>; an AI may not authorize spending on the owner\'s behalf' };
    92	  }
    93	  const cap = Number(opts.capUsd);
    94	  if (!Number.isFinite(cap) || cap <= 0) {
    95	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --cap-usd <number>; authorization without a cap is unbounded' };
    96	  }
    97	  const ceiling = Number(b.hardCeilingUsd);
    98	  if (Number.isFinite(ceiling) && cap > ceiling) {
    99	    return { ok: false, reason: `--cap-usd ${cap} exceeds the policy hard ceiling of ${ceiling}` };
   100	  }
   101	  return { ok: true, approvedBy: String(opts.approvedBy), capUsd: cap };
   102	}
   103	
   104	// Role routing: who performs a role, with the separation rules enforced.
   105	function routeRole(roleId, opts = {}) {
   106	  const policy = loadPolicy();
   107	  const role = (policy.roles || {})[roleId];
   108	  if (!role) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_ROLE', reason: `${roleId} is not a role in the routing policy.` };
   109	
   110	  const chosen = opts.model || role.default;
   111	
   112	  // A reviewer that is also the author is not a reviewer.
   113	  for (const other of role.mustDifferFrom || []) {
   114	    const otherRole = (policy.roles || {})[other];
   115	    const otherModel = (opts.assigned && opts.assigned[other]) || (otherRole && otherRole.default);
   116	    if (otherModel && otherModel === chosen) {
   117	      return {
   118	        ok: false, code: 'SELF_REVIEW_REFUSED',
   119	        reason: `${roleId} would be performed by "${chosen}", which is already performing "${other}". A model may not review its own work.`,
   120	      };
   121	    }
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  // Recursive delegation is off unless the role explicitly opts in.
   125	  const recursion = role.recursiveDelegation === true;
   126	  if (opts.wantsDelegation && !recursion) {
   127	    return {
   128	      ok: false, code: 'RECURSIVE_DELEGATION_REFUSED',
   129	      reason: `${roleId} requested sub-agent delegation, which is disabled by default. One bounded budget must not become an unbounded tree.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const model = (policy.models || {})[chosen];
   134	  if (!model) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_MODEL', reason: `${chosen} is not declared in the routing policy.` };
   135	
   136	  // Data sensitivity vetoes first, before capability or cost are considered.
   137	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   138	  const rank = (policy.dataClasses || {});
   139	  const want = rank[dataClass] && rank[dataClass].rank;
   140	  const max = rank[model.maxDataClass] && rank[model.maxDataClass].rank;
   141	  if (typeof want === 'number' && typeof max === 'number' && want > max) {
   142	    return {
   143	      ok: false, code: 'DATA_CLASS_REFUSED',
   144	      reason: `${dataClass} data may not be sent to ${chosen} (max ${model.maxDataClass}). Data sensitivity vetoes before capability or cost.`,
   145	    };
   146	  }
   147	
   148	  if (model.execution === 'METERED') {
   149	    const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   150	    if (!m.ok) return { ok: false, code: 'METERED_UNAUTHORIZED', reason: m.reason };
   151	  }
   152	
   153	  return {
   154	    ok: true, roleId, model: chosen, label: model.label,
   155	    execution: model.execution, dataClass,
   156	    bounds: role.bounds || null,
   157	    recursiveDelegation: recursion,
   158	    advisoryOnly: role.advisoryOnly === true,
   159	    mayApproveOwnWork: role.mayApproveOwnWork === true,
   160	  };
   161	}
   162	
   163	function route(canon, opts) {
   164	  const task = canon.taskTypes.find((t) => t.taskId === opts.task);
   165	  if (!task) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_TASK', reason: `Task ${opts.task} is not in the Tool Capability Canon.` };
   166	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   167	  if (!(dataClass in DATA_RANK)) return { ok: false, code: 'INVALID_DATA_CLASS', reason: `Unknown data class ${dataClass}.` };
   168	
   169	  const byId = new Map(canon.tools.map((t) => [t.toolId, t]));
   170	  const considered = [];
   171	  for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds) {
   172	    const tool = byId.get(toolId);
   173	    if (!tool) continue;
   174	    const reasons = [];
   175	    if (!tool.enabled || tool.availability === 'DISABLED') reasons.push('disabled');
   176	    if (tool.availability !== 'AVAILABLE') reasons.push(`availability=${tool.availability}`);
   177	    if (DATA_RANK[dataClass] > DATA_RANK[tool.maxDataClassification]) reasons.push(`data class ${dataClass} exceeds ${tool.maxDataClassification}`);
   178	    // CONFIRMED FINDING #5: a bare --allow-metered boolean used to authorize
   179	    // spending. Money now requires a name attached to the decision and a
   180	    // number attached to the spend, both bounded by the policy ceiling.
   181	    if (tool.costClass === 'METERED') {
   182	      const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   183	      if (!m.ok) reasons.push(m.reason);
   184	    }
   185	    considered.push({ toolId, eligible: reasons.length === 0, reasons });
   186	    if (reasons.length === 0) {
   187	      return {
   188	        ok: true,
   189	        taskId: task.taskId,
   190	        toolId: tool.toolId,
   191	        label: tool.label,
   192	        executionMode: tool.executionModes[0],
   193	        costClass: tool.costClass,
   194	        dataClass,
   195	        verification: tool.verification,
   196	        requiredInputs: task.requiredInputs || [],
   197	        requiredOutputs: task.requiredOutputs || [],
   198	        considered,
   199	      };
   200	    }
   201	  }
   202	  return {
   203	    ok: false,
   204	    code: 'SPECIALIST_REQUIRED',
   205	    reason: `No verified, enabled specialist can execute ${task.taskId}. Generalist fallback is ${task.generalistFallback}.`,
   206	    taskId: task.taskId,
   207	    considered,
   208	  };
   209	}
   210	
   211	function main() {
   212	  const opts = parse(process.argv.slice(2));
   213	  if (opts.unknown) {
   214	    console.error(`Unknown argument: ${opts.unknown}`);
   215	    return EXIT_USAGE;
   216	  }
   217	  const canonPath = path.resolve(ROOT, opts.canon || path.relative(ROOT, DEFAULT_CANON));
   218	  let canon;
   219	  try { canon = readCanon(canonPath); }
   220	  catch (e) { console.error(`Cannot read canon ${canonPath}: ${e.message}`); return EXIT_USAGE; }
   221	  const errors = validate(canon);
   222	  if (errors.length) {
   223	    console.error('TOOL CANON INVALID');
   224	    for (const error of errors) console.error(`- ${error}`);
   225	    return EXIT_USAGE;

   160	// ── reviewers (from the tool canon — availability with dated evidence) ──────
   161	function projectReviewers(now) {
   162	  if (!fs.existsSync(CANON)) {
   163	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon not found at ${rel(CANON)}`, reviewers: [] };
   164	  }
   165	  let canon;
   166	  try { canon = readJSON(CANON); }
   167	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}`, reviewers: [] }; }
   168	
   169	  const wanted = { 'codex-local': 'independent review', 'grok-cli': 'adversarial red team' };
   170	  const reviewers = [];
   171	  for (const [toolId, role] of Object.entries(wanted)) {
   172	    const t = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === toolId);
   173	    if (!t) {
   174	      reviewers.push({ toolId, role, availability: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   175	        reason: 'not present in the Tool Capability Canon', evidence: null, source: rel(CANON) });
   176	      continue;
   177	    }
   178	    const ev = t.availabilityEvidence || null;
   179	    reviewers.push({
   180	      toolId, role,
   181	      label: t.label || toolId,
   182	      availability: t.availability || 'UNKNOWN',
   183	      enabled: t.enabled !== false,
   184	      evidence: ev ? { observedAt: ev.observedAt, method: ev.method, result: ev.result } : null,
   185	      evidenceAgeMinutes: ev && ev.observedAt ? ageMinutes(ev.observedAt, now) : null,
   186	      source: rel(CANON),
   187	    });
   188	  }
   189	  return { state: 'OK', reviewers };
   190	}

   192	// ── engineering state (the gate's own verdict — never re-judged here) ───────
   193	function projectEngineering(args) {
   194	  const a = [ENGOS, '--gate-done', '--json'];
   195	  if (args.packet) a.push('--packet', args.packet);
   196	  if (args.subjectSha) a.push('--subject-sha', args.subjectSha);
   197	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   198	
   199	  // A BLOCKED gate exits 3. That is a successful reading of a blocked system,
   200	  // not a failure to read — so it is projected, not swallowed.
   201	  let parsed = null;
   202	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* handled below */ }
   203	  if (!parsed) {
   204	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   205	      reason: `could not read a gate verdict (exit ${r.status}): ${(r.stderr || '').trim().split('\n')[0] || 'no output'}`,
   206	      source: rel(ENGOS) };
   207	  }
   208	
   209	  const stageEvidence = deriveStages(parsed);
   210	  return {
   211	    state: 'OK',
   212	    gateExit: r.status,
   213	    verdict: parsed.ok ? parsed.state : 'BLOCKED',
   214	    lane: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : 'UNKNOWN',
   215	    highRisk: parsed.classification ? !!parsed.classification.highRisk : null,
   216	    requiredReviewers: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.requiredReviewers : [],
   217	    subjectSha256: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectSha256 : null,
   218	    subjectPaths: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectPaths : [],
   219	    excludedAsEvidence: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.excludedAsEvidence : [],
   220	    problems: parsed.problems || [],
   221	    observed: parsed.observed || [],
   222	    unverified: parsed.unverified || [],
   223	    stages: stageEvidence,
   224	    source: rel(ENGOS),
   225	  };
   226	}
   227	
   228	// Map the gate verdict onto the declared stage sequence. This does not decide
   229	// anything — it re-expresses the gate's own problems as stage states so the
   230	// drift is drawable. A stage with no positive evidence is UNVERIFIED, which is
   231	// distinct from FAILED: nobody looked, versus somebody looked and it failed.
   232	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   233	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   234	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   235	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   236	
   237	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   238	    switch (id) {
   239	      case 'spec':
   240	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   241	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   242	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   243	      case 'build':
   244	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   245	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   246	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   247	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   248	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   249	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   250	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   251	        }
   252	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   253	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   254	        }
   255	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   256	      case 'deterministic':
   257	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   258	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   260	      case 'codex':
   261	      case 'grok': {
   262	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   263	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   264	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   265	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   266	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   267	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   268	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   269	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   270	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   271	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   272	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   273	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   274	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   275	        }
   276	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   277	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   278	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   279	        }
   280	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   281	      }
   282	      case 'copilot':
   283	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   284	      case 'gate':
   285	        return parsed.ok
   286	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   287	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   288	      case 'runtime':
   289	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   290	      case 'checkpoint':
   291	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   292	      default:
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   294	    }
   295	  };
   296	
   297	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   298	}
   299	
   300	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   301	function projectEvents(limit) {
   302	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   303	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   304	  }
   305	  let l;
   306	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   307	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   308	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   309	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   310	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   311	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   312	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   313	    operation: e.operation || null,
   314	  }));
   315	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };
   316	}
   317	
   318	// ── snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   319	function snapshot(args) {
   320	  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();
   321	  const now = Date.parse(nowIso);
   322	  const engineering = projectEngineering(args);
   323	  const integration = { connectors: projectConnectors(now) };
   324	
   325	  // Failure isolation, made structural: these are two objects. There is no
   326	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   327	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   328	  return {
   329	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   330	    contract: {
   331	      absences: ['UNAVAILABLE', 'STALE', 'UNVERIFIED'],
   332	      note: 'Engineering and integration state are separate and are never blended. No field in this snapshot is synthesised; every value cites the artifact it came from.',
   333	    },
   334	    engineering,
   335	    integration,
   336	    // A THIRD plane, deliberately not merged into either of the others. It is
   337	    // not engineering (it holds no authority) and not integration (a Notion
   338	    // disagreement is not a connector fault). Separating it is precisely what
   339	    // makes "engineering VERIFIED / knowledge CONFLICT" a renderable pair
   340	    // rather than a contradiction something has to resolve.

    91	const exists = (root, rel) => fs.existsSync(path.join(root, rel));

    93	function auditSource() {
    94	  return {
    95	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, d.p) })),
    96	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, p) })),
    97	  };
    98	}
    99	
   100	function auditTarget(target) {
   101	  return {
   102	    isDir: fs.existsSync(target) && fs.statSync(target).isDirectory(),
   103	    isGit: fs.existsSync(path.join(target, '.git')),
   104	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(target, d.p) })),
   105	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(target, p) })),
   106	  };
   107	}

     1	#!/usr/bin/env node
     2	/**
     3	 * ledger-writer.cjs  — Builder Control System v1, Module 3
     4	 *
     5	 * Usage:
     6	 *   --append <entry.json>          Validate entry against ledger-entry.schema.json,
     7	 *                                  then append it to builder-control/ledger.json.
     8	 *                                  NEVER mutates or truncates existing entries.
     9	 *   --migrate-policy-ledger        Migrate the single policy/ledger.json record into
    10	 *                                  builder-control/ledger.json as a back-dated IMPORTED
    11	 *                                  entry. Leaves policy/ledger.json as a stub.
    12	 *                                  Safe to re-run: idempotent (checks if already migrated).
    13	 *
    14	 * Invariant: the ledger file is a JSON array. Every operation that touches the file
    15	 * reads the current array, validates no mutation occurred, then writes
    16	 * [  ...existingEntries, newEntry  ]. It never truncates.
    17	 *
    18	 * No npm deps — only Node built-ins + shared hand-rolled JSON-Schema validator.
    19	 */
    20	'use strict';
    21	
    22	const fs   = require('fs');
    23	const path = require('path');
    24	
    25	// ─── Paths ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    26	const LEDGER_FILE   = path.join(__dirname, 'ledger.json');
    27	const SCHEMA_FILE   = path.join(__dirname, 'schemas', 'ledger-entry.schema.json');
    28	const POLICY_LEDGER = path.join(__dirname, '..', 'policy', 'ledger.json');

    30	// ─── Hand-rolled JSON-Schema draft-07 validator (same logic as packet-tools) ─
    31	function validateSchema(schema, data, ctx, defs) {
    32	  ctx  = ctx  || '#';
    33	  defs = defs || schema['$defs'] || schema['definitions'] || {};
    34	
    35	  const errors = [];
    36	
    37	  if (schema['$ref']) {
    38	    const ref = schema['$ref'];
    39	    if (ref.startsWith('#/$defs/')) {
    40	      const defKey = ref.slice('#/$defs/'.length);
    41	      if (!defs[defKey]) { errors.push(`${ctx}: unresolved $ref ${ref}`); return errors; }
    42	      return validateSchema(defs[defKey], data, ctx, defs);
    43	    }
    44	    errors.push(`${ctx}: unsupported $ref ${ref}`);
    45	    return errors;
    46	  }
    47	
    48	  if (schema.type) {
    49	    const types = Array.isArray(schema.type) ? schema.type : [schema.type];
    50	    const actual = data === null ? 'null' : Array.isArray(data) ? 'array' : typeof data;
    51	    // JSON-Schema: 'integer' means a number with no fractional part
    52	    const typesForCheck = types.map(t => t === 'integer' ? 'number' : t);
    53	    if (!typesForCheck.includes(actual)) {
    54	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected type ${types.join('|')} but got ${actual}`);
    55	      return errors;
    56	    }
    57	    // If type is 'integer', also verify it's a whole number
    58	    if (types.includes('integer') && actual === 'number' && !Number.isInteger(data)) {
    59	      errors.push(`${ctx}: expected integer but got non-integer number ${data}`);
    60	      return errors;
    61	    }
    62	  }
    63	
    64	  if (schema.required && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    65	    for (const req of schema.required) {
    66	      if (!(req in data)) errors.push(`${ctx}: missing required property "${req}"`);
    67	    }
    68	  }
    69	
    70	  if (schema.properties && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    71	    for (const [key, subSchema] of Object.entries(schema.properties)) {
    72	      if (key in data) {
    73	        errors.push(...validateSchema(subSchema, data[key], `${ctx}/${key}`, defs));
    74	      }
    75	    }
    76	  }
    77	
    78	  if (schema.additionalProperties === false && typeof data === 'object' && data !== null && !Array.isArray(data)) {
    79	    const allowed = new Set(Object.keys(schema.properties || {}));
    80	    for (const key of Object.keys(data)) {
    81	      if (!allowed.has(key)) errors.push(`${ctx}: unexpected additional property "${key}"`);
    82	    }
    83	  }
    84	
    85	  if (schema.pattern && typeof data === 'string') {
    86	    if (!new RegExp(schema.pattern).test(data)) {
    87	      errors.push(`${ctx}: string does not match pattern ${schema.pattern}`);
    88	    }
    89	  }
    90	
    91	  if (schema.enum && !schema.enum.includes(data)) {
    92	    errors.push(`${ctx}: must be one of ${JSON.stringify(schema.enum)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
    93	  }
    94	
    95	  if (typeof schema.minItems === 'number' && Array.isArray(data) && data.length < schema.minItems) {
    96	    errors.push(`${ctx}: array has ${data.length} items, minimum is ${schema.minItems}`);
    97	  }
    98	
    99	  if (schema.items && Array.isArray(data)) {
   100	    data.forEach((item, idx) => {
   101	      errors.push(...validateSchema(schema.items, item, `${ctx}[${idx}]`, defs));
   102	    });
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  if ('const' in schema && data !== schema.const) {
   106	    errors.push(`${ctx}: expected const ${JSON.stringify(schema.const)}, got ${JSON.stringify(data)}`);
   107	  }
   108	
   109	  return errors;
   110	}
   111	
   112	// ─── Load JSON file ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   113	function loadJSON(filePath) {
   114	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8'));
   115	}
   116	
   117	// ─── Read the current ledger array (creates empty array if file absent) ────────
   118	function readLedger() {
   119	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) return [];
   120	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, 'utf8').trim();
   121	  if (!raw) return [];
   122	  const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
   123	  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {
   124	    throw new Error(`Ledger at ${LEDGER_FILE} is not a JSON array — aborting to prevent data loss`);
   125	  }
   126	  return parsed;
   127	}
   128	
   129	// ─── Write the ledger, asserting old entries are byte-identical ───────────────
   130	function writeLedger(oldEntries, newEntry) {
   131	  const newArray  = [...oldEntries, newEntry];
   132	
   133	  // Safety: re-read and verify old entries are unchanged
   134	  if (fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) {
   135	    const onDisk = readLedger();
   136	    if (onDisk.length < oldEntries.length) {
   137	      throw new Error('LEDGER INTEGRITY VIOLATION: on-disk array is shorter than expected — refusing write');
   138	    }
   139	    // Byte-compare each old entry
   140	    for (let i = 0; i < oldEntries.length; i++) {
   141	      if (JSON.stringify(onDisk[i]) !== JSON.stringify(oldEntries[i])) {
   142	        throw new Error(`LEDGER INTEGRITY VIOLATION: entry at index ${i} differs on disk vs in memory — another process may have written concurrently`);
   143	      }
   144	    }
   145	  }
   146	
   147	  fs.writeFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, JSON.stringify(newArray, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   148	  return newArray.length;
   149	}
   150	
   151	// ─── Validate an entry object against the ledger-entry schema ─────────────────
   152	function validateEntry(entry) {
   153	  const schema = loadJSON(SCHEMA_FILE);
   154	  const defs   = schema['$defs'] || {};
   155	  return validateSchema(schema, entry, '#', defs);
   156	}
   157	
   158	// ─── --append <entry.json> ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   159	function appendEntry(entryPath) {
   160	  // Load entry
   161	  let entry;
   162	  try {
   163	    entry = loadJSON(path.resolve(entryPath));
   164	  } catch (e) {
   165	    console.error(`ERROR loading entry file: ${e.message}`);
   166	    process.exit(2);
   167	  }
   168	
   169	  // Validate
   170	  const errors = validateEntry(entry);
   171	  if (errors.length) {
   172	    console.error('SCHEMA VALIDATION FAILED for entry:');
   173	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   174	    process.exit(1);
   175	  }
   176	
   177	  // Append
   178	  const before = readLedger();
   179	  const newLen = writeLedger(before, entry);
   180	
   181	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Appended entry "${entry.entryId}" to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   182	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${before.length} → ${newLen}  (grew by 1)`);
   183	  process.exit(0);
   184	}
   185

   187	function migratePolicyLedger() {

   200	  }
   201

   204	    console.log('[ledger-writer] policy/ledger.json is already a stub — migration already complete.');
   205	    process.exit(0);
   206	  }
   207	
   208	  // 2. Extract the single record
   209	  // Shape: { "agent:main:main": { artifacts: { research_report: [ { ts, source: { via, targetAgent, targetRole } } ] } } }
   210	  const agentKey   = 'agent:main:main';

     1	# AEGIS V1 — Owner and Architecture Contract
     2	
     3	**Owner:** Marc Papineau  
     4	**Approved:** 2026-08-23  
     5	**Status:** APPROVED FOR INCREMENTAL BUILD  
     6	**Scope:** A generic local-first build operating system. It is not a feature of
     7	any one product.
     8	
     9	## 1. Outcome
    10	
    11	AEGIS gives the Product Owner control over AI-assisted builds without requiring
    12	him to read long agent transcripts. Within roughly ten seconds he should know:
    13	
    14	- what is being built and against which approved requirement;
    15	- the current workflow state, agent, model, tool, and execution mode;
    16	- what failed, what is blocked, and what needs a human decision;
    17	- which checks and reviews actually ran and where their evidence lives;
    18	- whether metered spending occurred;
    19	- the last known-good checkpoint and available rollback action.
    20	
    21	The dashboard observes and controls the canonical Workflow Engine. **It does
    22	not own or duplicate workflow state.**
    23	
    24	## 2. Local-first boundary
    25	
    26	The following live on Marc's machine in V1:
    27	
    28	- dashboard application and local control service;
    29	- durable workflow, policy, event, approval, and evidence records;
    30	- SQLite database in WAL mode;
    31	- repositories, isolated worktrees, specifications, and local artifacts;
    32	- watchdog, heartbeats, leases, stale-run detection, and recovery metadata;
    33	- tool/model configuration and budget policy;
    34	- credential references. Secret values belong in macOS Keychain, never the DB,
    35	  prompts, event log, screenshots, or repository.
    36	
    37	GitHub, Notion, and cloud AI/media tools remain external services. Local-first
    38	does not mean local inference: relevant context may leave the machine when an
    39	external provider is used. The router must enforce data-classification limits
    40	and record the provider selected.
    41	
    42	## 3. Durable control plane
    43	
    44	The orchestrator does not remember the state. A durable state machine owns it.
    45	Every transition records actor, timestamp, subject, evidence, and policy
    46	decision. Agents request transitions; policy permits or refuses them.
    47	
    48	Minimum states:
    49	
    50	`PENDING -> ANALYZING -> PLANNED -> IMPLEMENTING -> DETERMINISTIC_VALIDATION ->
    51	INDEPENDENT_REVIEW -> CORRECTION -> RUNTIME_VALIDATION -> CHECKPOINTING ->
    52	VERIFIED`
    53	
    54	`BLOCKED`, `PAUSED`, `CANCELLED`, and `RECOVERY_REQUIRED` are explicit states.
    55	Retries are idempotent. A lease prevents two workers owning one step. A
    56	heartbeat and timeout expose abandoned work. Resume starts from durable state,
    57	not conversation memory.
    58	
    59	## 4. Execution modes and money
    60	
    61	Every executable capability declares one mode:
    62	
    63	1. `SUBSCRIPTION_CLI` — preferred when a supported authenticated CLI uses an
    64	   existing subscription allowance.
    65	2. `BUILT_IN` or `PLUGIN_MCP` — preferred when already available in the active
    66	   surface and suitable for the task.
    67	3. `DESKTOP_ASSISTED` — for attended or semi-attended work such as documents,
    68	   Notion, or a provider UI. It is not trusted as the only unattended control
    69	   path.
    70	4. `API_METERED` — reliable headless execution when authorized.
    71	5. `UNAVAILABLE` — honest terminal state when nothing appropriate can run.
    72	
    73	Default metered budget is **$0**. A non-zero metered run requires a named human
    74	approval and a hard cap. Included-plan usage is still tracked because plan
    75	limits are finite.
    76	
    77	## 5. Capability-first tool routing
    78	
    79	AEGIS routes the task to a verified capability before choosing a model.
    80	
    81	- Claude is not a video generator, advertising platform, design application,
    82	  deployment host, or spreadsheet engine merely because it can describe one.
    83	- A specialist-required task cannot fall back silently to a general model.
    84	- Video generation prefers OpenArt when its connector is verified and enabled.
    85	- Advertising requires an enabled advertising specialist selected in the Tool
    86	  Capability Canon. Until one is connected, advertising execution blocks.
    87	- Attachments, MCPs, plugins, APIs, CLIs, desktop tools, and future add-ons are
    88	  all connector types behind the same selection contract.
    89	- Tools may be enabled, disabled, reprioritized, or replaced without rewriting
    90	  workflow doctrine.
    91	
    92	Selection considers task match, availability evidence, data classification,
    93	cost authorization, required inputs, output type, and owner preference. If no
    94	eligible specialist exists, return `SPECIALIST_REQUIRED`, not an improvised
    95	output.
    96	
    97	## 6. Lean workflow
    98	
    99	The ordering rule is **cheapest reliable falsifier first**:
   100	
   101	`objective -> pinned acceptance criteria -> risk -> isolated worktree -> build
   102	-> syntax/type/lint/unit/build checks -> independent review -> bounded
   103	correction -> checks again -> PR/CI -> repository review -> runtime evidence ->
   104	risk-gated red team -> watchdog -> checkpoint -> verified documentation sync`
   105	
   106	Grok is required for high-risk, novel, security-sensitive, control-system, and
   107	milestone work. It is not called for harmless formatting or tiny documentation
   108	changes. Maximum normal correction cycles: three.
   109	
   110	## 7. Tool Capability Canon
   111	
   112	`builder-control/TOOL-CAPABILITY-CANON.json` is the versioned seed catalog for
   113	task-to-tool policy. The live dashboard will store current probe results in its
   114	local DB, but may not silently rewrite the canon.
   115	
   116	Each tool profile names:
   117	
   118	- supported task types and capabilities;
   119	- preferred execution modes;
   120	- availability and evidence;
   121	- strengths and explicit exclusions;
   122	- required inputs and output artifacts;
   123	- cost class, approval rule, and data-classification ceiling;
   124	- verification required before its output is accepted.
   125	
   126	## 8. V1 vertical slice
   127	
   128	V1 proves one complete local loop:
   129	
   130	1. text objective entered;
   131	2. acceptance criteria and risk recorded;
   132	3. tool/model route selected from verified capabilities;
   133	4. isolated worktree created;
   134	5. builder runs;
   135	6. deterministic checks run;
   136	7. independent review binds to the exact subject;
   137	8. corrections are bounded and rechecked;
   138	9. watchdog proves the policy sequence;
   139	10. checkpoint and rollback point recorded;
   140	11. dashboard displays evidence, costs, blockers, and controls.
   141	
   142	Notion begins as approved-intake plus one-way post-checkpoint synchronization.
   143	Bi-directional conflict resolution, voice, cinematic visualization, historical
   144	model learning, and dynamic optimization are later versions.
   145	
   146	## 9. Anti-theatre acceptance
   147	
   148	AEGIS V1 is not complete until the vertical slice is executed end-to-end and a
   149	deliberate negative case proves each control blocks: missing specialist, stale
   150	review, unavailable builder, failed deterministic check, skipped workflow step,
   151	unauthorized metered spend, stale lease, and rollback from a known-good point.
   152

    58	A connector may **execute** `SYNC_VERIFIED_FEATURE_TO_NOTION`. A connector may
    59	never **decide** that the feature is verified.
    60	
    61	Concretely forbidden to every connector: marking work VERIFIED · bypassing a
    62	stage · approving its own execution · modifying policy · removing a review gate ·
    63	declaring tests or runtime PASS · suppressing findings · changing product truth ·
    64	creating a checkpoint · overriding the watchdog or a human gate.
    65	
    66	This is enforced mechanically: the projector refuses to load a registry entry
    67	whose `plane` is not `INTEGRATION`, and a test asserts a control-plane connector
    68	is rejected.
    69	
    70	## 4. Connector abstraction
    71	
    72	Every connector declares which operations it supports — no operation is assumed:
    73	
    74	`CONNECT · DISCONNECT · HEALTH_CHECK · READ · WRITE · SEARCH · CREATE · UPDATE ·
    75	DELETE · TRIGGER · STATUS`
    76	
    77	and reports: `status · authStatus · lastHealthCheck · lastSuccess · lastFailure ·
    78	failureCount · latencyMs · rateLimit · permissions · dependentWorkflows ·
    79	environment · riskLevel`.
    80	
    81	Health vocabulary: `HEALTHY · DEGRADED · FAILED · DISCONNECTED · AUTH_EXPIRED ·
    82	RATE_LIMITED · UNKNOWN`. **`UNKNOWN` is the default** — a connector that has
    83	never been probed is UNKNOWN, never HEALTHY. Optimism by default is how an
    84	outage stays invisible.
    85	
    86	## 5. Integration router — preference order
    87	
    88	For a required external action, choose the **smallest reliable path**:
    89	
    90	1. **Direct API** — preferred for a simple, reliable, single-system action.
    91	2. **Built-in / plugin / MCP** — when already authenticated and in-process.
    92	3. **Automation platform (Make)** — only for genuinely multi-system business
    93	   workflows.
    94	4. **n8n — LEGACY/OPTIONAL.** Not a default substrate. It is dead in this
    95	   estate (zero completed jobs since 2026-05-18) and must never be assumed live.
    96	
    97	Selection inputs: capability · availability with evidence · permissions ·
    98	historical failure rate · latency · security/data class · cost.
    99	
   100	**Never route a simple direct API call through an automation chain.** Each hop
   101	is a place the action can fail silently and a place the truth can be laundered.
   102	
   103	## 6. Failure isolation
   104	
   105	Engineering state and integration state are separate fields, always:
   106	
   107	```
   108	ENGINEERING STATUS : READY_FOR_PR      (control plane)
   109	DOCUMENTATION SYNC : FAILED            (integration plane)
   110	CONNECTOR          : notion
   111	RETRY              : AVAILABLE
   112	```
   113	
   114	A failed connector **never** downgrades verified engineering. A failed sync is
   115	**never** rendered as successful. Two states, two fields, no blending.
   116	
   117	## 7. Idempotency
   118	
   119	Every meaningful external operation carries `operationId` (deterministic from
   120	objective + workflow + target), `correlationId`, `attempt`, `ts`, `result`.
   121	A retry with the same `operationId` is a no-op at the target. Destructive
   122	operations are never blindly repeated.
   123	
   124	## 8. Events
   125	
   126	Connector and workflow events are **ledger entries**, not a new store:
   127	`gate: "integration"`, `status: PASS|BLOCKED|FAILED`, plus operation ids.
   128	This inherits append-only semantics, the existing schema, and the existing
   129	audit tooling for free.
   130	
   131	## 9. Live topology + drift visualisation
   132	
   133	Nodes render only for systems **actually configured**. Node state is derived
   134	from real sources and may be: `PENDING · ACTIVE · PASS · WARNING · FAILED ·
   135	BLOCKED · WAITING_FOR_HUMAN · UNAVAILABLE · DEGRADED · UNKNOWN`.
   136	
   137	**Process drift** is the signature view: the required stage sequence is declared,
   138	the actual evidence is compared against it, and any stage with no evidence is
   139	rendered as a break in the path with the missing gate named. This is not a
   140	decoration — it is the gate's own `--gate-done --json` output drawn as a line.
   141	
   142	## 10. Test plan
   143	
   144	| test | proves |
   145	|---|---|

   343	function runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec) {
   344	  const t = TOOLS[reviewer];
   345	  if (!t) return { ok: false, reason: `unknown reviewer ${reviewer}` };
   346	  const gateResult = canonGate(reviewer);
   347	  if (!gateResult.ok) return { ok: false, reason: gateResult.reason, raw: '' };
   348	  if (!isExecutable(t.bin)) {
   349	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} is not executable at ${t.bin}`, raw: '' };
   350	  }
   351	  let argv;
   352	  if (reviewer === 'codex') {
   353	    // read-only sandbox; the model cannot write regardless of what it decides.
   354	    argv = ['exec', '--sandbox', 'read-only', '-C', ROOT, '--skip-git-repo-check', prompt];
   355	  } else {
   356	    // Bounds come from MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json, not from this file, so the
   357	    // Product Owner can tighten an adversarial reviewer without editing code.
   358	    // Single-turn print mode, no approval flags, no web, no sub-agents: the
   359	    // adversary gets exactly one pass at the diff and no way to spend the
   360	    // budget on a conversation with itself.
   361	    argv = ['-p', prompt, '--output-format', 'json', '--cwd', ROOT];
   362	    let bounds = null;
   363	    try {
   364	      bounds = (require('./tool-router.cjs').loadPolicy().roles['adversarial-review'] || {}).bounds || null;
   365	    } catch { /* absent policy is handled by the router's own refusal */ }
   366	    // CONFIRMED DEFECT (2026-08-24, caught by reading a real run's telemetry):
   367	    // the policy declared maxTurns:1 and this adapter never passed it. Grok ran
   368	    // 13 turns and returned no record. A bound that is declared in a policy but
   369	    // not passed to the tool is theatre — it reads as governance in the JSON and
   370	    // enforces nothing at runtime. Every bound below is now wired to a real flag
   371	    // or refuses, and buildToolArgv() is asserted against the policy by test.
   372	    if (bounds && Number.isInteger(bounds.maxTurns) && bounds.maxTurns > 0) {
   373	      argv.push('--max-turns', String(bounds.maxTurns));
   374	    }
   375	    if (!bounds || bounds.webAccess === false) argv.push('--disable-web-search');
   376	    // Sub-agents are disabled by OMITTING --agents entirely. Passing an empty
   377	    // value was a bug: the CLI expects a map and rejected the array outright,
   378	    // which failed the launch at argument parsing. No definitions supplied is
   379	    // the correct way to express "no sub-agents".
   380	    if (bounds && bounds.subagents === true) {
   381	      throw new Error('policy enables sub-agents for adversarial-review; this adapter deliberately does not implement that path');
   382	    }
   383	  }
   384	  const r = spawnSync(t.bin, argv, {
   385	    encoding: 'utf8',
   386	    timeout: timeoutSec * 1000,
   387	    maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024,
   388	    cwd: ROOT,
   389	  });
   390	  const raw = (r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr ? `\n--- stderr ---\n${r.stderr}` : '');
   391	  if (r.error && r.error.code === 'ETIMEDOUT') {
   392	    return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exceeded the ${timeoutSec}s timeout`, raw };
   393	  }
   394	  if (r.error) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} failed to start: ${r.error.message}`, raw };
   395	  if (r.status !== 0) return { ok: false, reason: `${t.label} exited ${r.status}`, raw };
   396	  return { ok: true, raw };
   397	}
   398	
   399	function cmdRun(args) {
   400	  const reviewer = args.reviewer;
   401	  if (!reviewer) return usage('--reviewer is required');
   402	  if (!['codex', 'grok', 'copilot'].includes(reviewer)) return usage(`unknown reviewer ${reviewer}`);
   403	  if (!args.packet) return usage('--packet is required');
   404	  if (!fs.existsSync(args.packet)) return usage(`packet not found: ${args.packet}`);
   405	
   406	  const packet = JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(args.packet, 'utf8'));
   407	  const subject = subjectOf(args);
   408	
   409	  if (args.subjectSha && args.subjectSha !== subject.subjectSha256) {
   410	    console.error(`[review-adapters] refusing to review: --subject-sha ${args.subjectSha.slice(0, 12)}… does not match the current subject ${subject.subjectSha256.slice(0, 12)}…`);
   411	    return EXIT_BLOCK;
   412	  }
   413	
   414	  fs.mkdirSync(REVIEWS_DIR, { recursive: true });
   415	  fs.mkdirSync(RAW_DIR, { recursive: true });
   416	  const ts = new Date().toISOString();
   417	  const stamp = ts.replace(/[^0-9]/g, '').slice(0, 14);
   418	
   419	  // Copilot has no local binary and no read-only path that avoids mutating a
   420	  // PR. During this pass GitHub mutation is forbidden outright, so the only
   421	  // honest output is UNAVAILABLE with the reason stated.
   422	  if (reviewer === 'copilot') {
   423	    const d = detect().copilot;
   424	    const record = buildRecord({
   425	      reviewer: 'copilot',
   426	      reviewerModel: 'github-copilot-code-review',
   427	      packetId: packet.packetId,
   428	      subject,
   429	      parsed: null,
   430	      unavailableReason:
   431	        `Copilot review could not be collected. ${d.detail}. Requesting a review requires an open pull request and a GitHub API mutation, which this correction pass forbids.`,
   432	      ts,
   433	    });
   434	    return writeRecord(record, `${stamp}-copilot`, '(no tool invoked — Copilot requires an open PR)');
   435	  }
   436	
   437	  const diff = subjectDiff(subject, args);
   438	  const prompt = reviewer === 'codex'
   439	    ? codexPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff)
   440	    : grokPrompt(packet.objective, subject, diff);
   441	
   442	  if (args.dryRun) {
   443	    console.log(`[review-adapters] DRY RUN — ${reviewer}: would send ${prompt.length} chars over ${subject.subjectPaths.length} subject path(s). No tool invoked.`);
   444	    return EXIT_PASS;
   445	  }
   446	
   447	  const timeoutSec = Number(args.timeout) > 0 ? Number(args.timeout) : 900;
   448	  const res = runTool(reviewer, prompt, timeoutSec);
   449	  const rawPath = path.join(RAW_DIR, `${stamp}-${reviewer}.txt`);
   450	  fs.writeFileSync(rawPath, res.raw || '(no output captured)', 'utf8');
   451	
   452	  const parsed = res.ok ? extractJson(res.raw) : null;
   453	  // When a tool exits 0 but returns no usable record, "no parseable JSON" is
   454	  // true but unhelpfully vague — and a vague UNAVAILABLE reason is the kind of
   455	  // thing an operator learns to ignore. Several CLIs report why they stopped

 succeeded in 0ms:
   155	    });
   156	  }
   157	  return { state: 'OK', thresholdMinutes: threshold, connectors };
   158	}

   160	// ── reviewers (from the tool canon — availability with dated evidence) ──────
   161	function projectReviewers(now) {
   162	  if (!fs.existsSync(CANON)) {
   163	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon not found at ${rel(CANON)}`, reviewers: [] };
   164	  }
   165	  let canon;
   166	  try { canon = readJSON(CANON); }
   167	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `tool canon unreadable: ${e.message}`, reviewers: [] }; }
   168	
   169	  const wanted = { 'codex-local': 'independent review', 'grok-cli': 'adversarial red team' };
   170	  const reviewers = [];
   171	  for (const [toolId, role] of Object.entries(wanted)) {
   172	    const t = (canon.tools || []).find((x) => x.toolId === toolId);
   173	    if (!t) {
   174	      reviewers.push({ toolId, role, availability: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   175	        reason: 'not present in the Tool Capability Canon', evidence: null, source: rel(CANON) });
   176	      continue;
   177	    }
   178	    const ev = t.availabilityEvidence || null;
   179	    reviewers.push({
   180	      toolId, role,
   181	      label: t.label || toolId,
   182	      availability: t.availability || 'UNKNOWN',
   183	      enabled: t.enabled !== false,
   184	      evidence: ev ? { observedAt: ev.observedAt, method: ev.method, result: ev.result } : null,
   185	      evidenceAgeMinutes: ev && ev.observedAt ? ageMinutes(ev.observedAt, now) : null,
   186	      source: rel(CANON),
   187	    });
   188	  }
   189	  return { state: 'OK', reviewers };
   190	}
   191	
   192	// ── engineering state (the gate's own verdict — never re-judged here) ───────
   193	function projectEngineering(args) {
   194	  const a = [ENGOS, '--gate-done', '--json'];
   195	  if (args.packet) a.push('--packet', args.packet);
   196	  if (args.subjectSha) a.push('--subject-sha', args.subjectSha);
   197	  const r = spawnSync('node', a, { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   198	
   199	  // A BLOCKED gate exits 3. That is a successful reading of a blocked system,
   200	  // not a failure to read — so it is projected, not swallowed.
   201	  let parsed = null;
   202	  try { parsed = JSON.parse(r.stdout); } catch { /* handled below */ }
   203	  if (!parsed) {
   204	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE',
   205	      reason: `could not read a gate verdict (exit ${r.status}): ${(r.stderr || '').trim().split('\n')[0] || 'no output'}`,
   206	      source: rel(ENGOS) };
   207	  }
   208	
   209	  const stageEvidence = deriveStages(parsed);
   210	  return {
   211	    state: 'OK',
   212	    gateExit: r.status,
   213	    verdict: parsed.ok ? parsed.state : 'BLOCKED',
   214	    lane: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : 'UNKNOWN',
   215	    highRisk: parsed.classification ? !!parsed.classification.highRisk : null,
   216	    requiredReviewers: parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.requiredReviewers : [],
   217	    subjectSha256: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectSha256 : null,
   218	    subjectPaths: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.subjectPaths : [],
   219	    excludedAsEvidence: parsed.subject ? parsed.subject.excludedAsEvidence : [],
   220	    problems: parsed.problems || [],
   221	    observed: parsed.observed || [],
   222	    unverified: parsed.unverified || [],
   223	    stages: stageEvidence,
   224	    source: rel(ENGOS),
   225	  };
   226	}
   227	
   228	// Map the gate verdict onto the declared stage sequence. This does not decide
   229	// anything — it re-expresses the gate's own problems as stage states so the
   230	// drift is drawable. A stage with no positive evidence is UNVERIFIED, which is
   231	// distinct from FAILED: nobody looked, versus somebody looked and it failed.
   232	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   233	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   234	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   235	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   236	
   237	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   238	    switch (id) {
   239	      case 'spec':
   240	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   241	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   242	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   243	      case 'build':
   244	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   245	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   246	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   247	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   248	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   249	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   250	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   251	        }
   252	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   253	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   254	        }
   255	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   256	      case 'deterministic':
   257	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   258	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   260	      case 'codex':
   261	      case 'grok': {
   262	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   263	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   264	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   265	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   266	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   267	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   268	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   269	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   270	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   271	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   272	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   273	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   274	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   275	        }
   276	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   277	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   278	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   279	        }
   280	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   281	      }
   282	      case 'copilot':
   283	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   284	      case 'gate':
   285	        return parsed.ok
   286	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   287	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   288	      case 'runtime':
   289	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   290	      case 'checkpoint':
   291	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   292	      default:
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   294	    }
   295	  };
   296	
   297	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   298	}
   299	
   300	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   301	function projectEvents(limit) {
   302	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   303	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   304	  }
   305	  let l;
   306	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   307	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   308	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   309	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   310	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   311	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   312	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   313	    operation: e.operation || null,
   314	  }));
   315	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };
   316	}

   326	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   327	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   328	  return {
   329	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   330	    contract: {
