    71	function loadPolicy() {
    72	  const fs = require('fs');
    73	  if (!fs.existsSync(POLICY_PATH)) {
    74	    const e = new Error('MODEL-ROUTING-POLICY.json is missing. Refusing to route: an absent policy is not a permissive policy.');
    75	    e.refused = true; throw e;
    76	  }
    77	  return JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(POLICY_PATH, 'utf8'));
    78	}
    79	
    80	// Metered execution needs a named human and a cap inside the hard ceiling.
    81	// Each condition exists because of a specific way spending goes wrong:
    82	// no name = nobody accountable; no cap = unbounded; cap above ceiling = the
    83	// ceiling was decorative.
    84	function meteredAuthorization(opts) {
    85	  let policy;
    86	  try { policy = loadPolicy(); }
    87	  catch (e) { return { ok: false, reason: 'model routing policy unreadable — metered execution refused' }; }
    88	  const b = policy.budgets || {};
    89	  if (!opts.allowMetered) return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution has no explicit budget authorization' };
    90	  if (!opts.approvedBy || String(opts.approvedBy).trim() === '') {
    91	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --approved-by <named human>; an AI may not authorize spending on the owner\'s behalf' };
    92	  }
    93	  const cap = Number(opts.capUsd);
    94	  if (!Number.isFinite(cap) || cap <= 0) {
    95	    return { ok: false, reason: 'metered execution requires --cap-usd <number>; authorization without a cap is unbounded' };
    96	  }
    97	  const ceiling = Number(b.hardCeilingUsd);
    98	  if (Number.isFinite(ceiling) && cap > ceiling) {
    99	    return { ok: false, reason: `--cap-usd ${cap} exceeds the policy hard ceiling of ${ceiling}` };
   100	  }
   101	  return { ok: true, approvedBy: String(opts.approvedBy), capUsd: cap };
   102	}
   103	
   104	// Role routing: who performs a role, with the separation rules enforced.
   105	function routeRole(roleId, opts = {}) {
   106	  const policy = loadPolicy();
   107	  const role = (policy.roles || {})[roleId];
   108	  if (!role) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_ROLE', reason: `${roleId} is not a role in the routing policy.` };
   109	
   110	  const chosen = opts.model || role.default;
   111	
   112	  // A reviewer that is also the author is not a reviewer.
   113	  for (const other of role.mustDifferFrom || []) {
   114	    const otherRole = (policy.roles || {})[other];
   115	    const otherModel = (opts.assigned && opts.assigned[other]) || (otherRole && otherRole.default);
   116	    if (otherModel && otherModel === chosen) {
   117	      return {
   118	        ok: false, code: 'SELF_REVIEW_REFUSED',
   119	        reason: `${roleId} would be performed by "${chosen}", which is already performing "${other}". A model may not review its own work.`,
   120	      };
   121	    }
   122	  }
   123	
   124	  // Recursive delegation is off unless the role explicitly opts in.
   125	  const recursion = role.recursiveDelegation === true;
   126	  if (opts.wantsDelegation && !recursion) {
   127	    return {
   128	      ok: false, code: 'RECURSIVE_DELEGATION_REFUSED',
   129	      reason: `${roleId} requested sub-agent delegation, which is disabled by default. One bounded budget must not become an unbounded tree.`,
   130	    };
   131	  }
   132	
   133	  const model = (policy.models || {})[chosen];
   134	  if (!model) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_MODEL', reason: `${chosen} is not declared in the routing policy.` };
   135	
   136	  // Data sensitivity vetoes first, before capability or cost are considered.
   137	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   138	  const rank = (policy.dataClasses || {});
   139	  const want = rank[dataClass] && rank[dataClass].rank;
   140	  const max = rank[model.maxDataClass] && rank[model.maxDataClass].rank;
   141	  if (typeof want === 'number' && typeof max === 'number' && want > max) {
   142	    return {
   143	      ok: false, code: 'DATA_CLASS_REFUSED',
   144	      reason: `${dataClass} data may not be sent to ${chosen} (max ${model.maxDataClass}). Data sensitivity vetoes before capability or cost.`,
   145	    };
   146	  }
   147	
   148	  if (model.execution === 'METERED') {
   149	    const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   150	    if (!m.ok) return { ok: false, code: 'METERED_UNAUTHORIZED', reason: m.reason };
   151	  }
   152	
   153	  return {
   154	    ok: true, roleId, model: chosen, label: model.label,
   155	    execution: model.execution, dataClass,
   156	    bounds: role.bounds || null,
   157	    recursiveDelegation: recursion,
   158	    advisoryOnly: role.advisoryOnly === true,
   159	    mayApproveOwnWork: role.mayApproveOwnWork === true,
   160	  };
   161	}
   162	
   163	function route(canon, opts) {
   164	  const task = canon.taskTypes.find((t) => t.taskId === opts.task);
   165	  if (!task) return { ok: false, code: 'UNKNOWN_TASK', reason: `Task ${opts.task} is not in the Tool Capability Canon.` };
   166	  const dataClass = opts.dataClass || 'INTERNAL';
   167	  if (!(dataClass in DATA_RANK)) return { ok: false, code: 'INVALID_DATA_CLASS', reason: `Unknown data class ${dataClass}.` };
   168	
   169	  const byId = new Map(canon.tools.map((t) => [t.toolId, t]));
   170	  const considered = [];
   171	  for (const toolId of task.preferredToolIds) {
   172	    const tool = byId.get(toolId);
   173	    if (!tool) continue;
   174	    const reasons = [];
   175	    if (!tool.enabled || tool.availability === 'DISABLED') reasons.push('disabled');
   176	    if (tool.availability !== 'AVAILABLE') reasons.push(`availability=${tool.availability}`);
   177	    if (DATA_RANK[dataClass] > DATA_RANK[tool.maxDataClassification]) reasons.push(`data class ${dataClass} exceeds ${tool.maxDataClassification}`);
   178	    // CONFIRMED FINDING #5: a bare --allow-metered boolean used to authorize
   179	    // spending. Money now requires a name attached to the decision and a
   180	    // number attached to the spend, both bounded by the policy ceiling.
   181	    if (tool.costClass === 'METERED') {
   182	      const m = meteredAuthorization(opts);
   183	      if (!m.ok) reasons.push(m.reason);
   184	    }
   185	    considered.push({ toolId, eligible: reasons.length === 0, reasons });
   186	    if (reasons.length === 0) {
   187	      return {
   188	        ok: true,
   189	        taskId: task.taskId,
   190	        toolId: tool.toolId,
   191	        label: tool.label,
   192	        executionMode: tool.executionModes[0],
   193	        costClass: tool.costClass,
   194	        dataClass,
   195	        verification: tool.verification,
   196	        requiredInputs: task.requiredInputs || [],
   197	        requiredOutputs: task.requiredOutputs || [],
   198	        considered,
   199	      };
   200	    }
   201	  }
   202	  return {
   203	    ok: false,
   204	    code: 'SPECIALIST_REQUIRED',
   205	    reason: `No verified, enabled specialist can execute ${task.taskId}. Generalist fallback is ${task.generalistFallback}.`,
   206	    taskId: task.taskId,
   207	    considered,
   208	  };
   209	}

    91	const exists = (root, rel) => fs.existsSync(path.join(root, rel));
    92	
    93	function auditSource() {
    94	  return {
    95	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, d.p) })),
    96	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(SOURCE_ROOT, p) })),
    97	  };
    98	}
    99	
   100	function auditTarget(target) {
   101	  return {
   102	    isDir: fs.existsSync(target) && fs.statSync(target).isDirectory(),
   103	    isGit: fs.existsSync(path.join(target, '.git')),
   104	    prereqs: PREREQS.map((d) => ({ ...d, present: exists(target, d.p) })),
   105	    osFiles: OS_FILES.map((p) => ({ p, present: exists(target, p) })),
   106	  };
   107	}
   108	
   109	function cmdDoctor(args) {
   110	  const src = auditSource();
   111	  const tgt = args.target ? auditTarget(args.target) : null;
   112	  const missingSrcPrereq = src.prereqs.filter((d) => !d.present);
   113	  const missingSrcOs = src.osFiles.filter((d) => !d.present);

   115	  if (args.json) {
   185	  for (const rel of all) {
   186	    const from = path.join(SOURCE_ROOT, rel);
   187	    const to = path.join(target, rel);
   188	    if (fs.existsSync(to) && !args.force) { skipped.push(rel); continue; }
   189	    fs.mkdirSync(path.dirname(to), { recursive: true });
   190	    fs.copyFileSync(from, to);
   191	    try { fs.chmodSync(to, fs.statSync(from).mode); } catch { /* best effort */ }
   192	    wrote.push(rel);
   193	  }
   194	
   195	  console.log('ENGINEERING OS — INSTALL');
   196	  console.log('='.repeat(64));
   197	  console.log(`target: ${target}`);
   198	  console.log(`copied : ${wrote.length}`);
   199	  for (const w of wrote) console.log(`  + ${w}`);
   200	  if (skipped.length) {
   201	    console.log(`kept   : ${skipped.length} existing file(s) left untouched (use --force to overwrite)`);
   202	    for (const s of skipped) console.log(`  = ${s}`);
   203	  }
   204	  console.log('');
   205	  console.log('NEXT — none of this is done for you, and none of it is claimed:');
   206	  console.log('  1. Verify:  node builder-control/test/engineering-os.test.cjs');
   207	  console.log('               node builder-control/test/tool-router.test.cjs');
   208	  console.log('  2. Copy the "Engineering OS —" steps into the target CI workflow.');
   209	  console.log('  3. Review protected-paths.json: it was copied from the source repo');
   210	  console.log('     and lists SOURCE paths. It must be rewritten for the target, or');
   211	  console.log('     the target will protect files it does not have and miss its own.');
   212	  console.log('  4. Reviewer binaries are machine-local and were NOT installed.');
   213	  console.log('     Run: node builder-control/review-adapters.cjs --doctor');
   214	  return EXIT_PASS;
   215	}

   217	function parseArgs(argv) {
   218	  const a = {};
   219	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   220	    const t = argv[i];
   221	    if (t === '--target') a.target = argv[++i];
   222	    else if (t === '--install') a.install = true;
   223	    else if (t === '--doctor') a.doctor = true;
   224	    else if (t === '--force') a.force = true;
   225	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   226	  }
   227	  return a;
   228	}

   752	  // 6. Supersession — the one documented deterministic replacement rule.
   753	  //    A record may declare `supersedes: <reviewId>`; the named record is
   754	  //    dropped. Anything else that leaves two records for the same reviewer on
   755	  //    the same subject is AMBIGUOUS and blocks, because picking a winner by

   807	  if (byReviewer['claude-self']) {
   808	    observed.push('claude-self record present (recorded for traceability; it satisfies no required slot)');
   809	  }
   810	  for (const need of cls.requiredReviewers) {
   811	    const recs = byReviewer[need] || [];
   812	    if (!recs.length) {
   813	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING', detail: `${need} is required for lane ${cls.lane}${cls.highRisk ? '/high-risk' : ''} and has no valid review record bound to this subject. Missing evidence is a BLOCK, never an implicit pass.` });
   814	      continue;
   815	    }
   816	    const unavailable = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'UNAVAILABLE');
   817	    if (unavailable) {
   818	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE', detail: `${need} reported UNAVAILABLE: ${unavailable.unavailableReason}. Reported honestly — and it still blocks, because this lane requires that review.` });
   819	      continue;
   820	    }
   821	    const rejected = recs.find((r) => r.disposition === 'REJECT');
   822	    if (rejected) { problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED', detail: `${need} returned REJECT (${rejected.reviewId})` }); continue; }
   823	    observed.push(`${need}: ${recs.map((r) => r.disposition).join(', ')}`);
   824	  }
   825	
   826	  // 10. Blocking findings — from ACTIVE, SUBJECT-BOUND records only.
   827	  const blocking = [];
   828	  for (const rec of active) {
   829	    for (const f of rec.findings || []) {
   830	      if ((f.severity === 'CRITICAL' || f.severity === 'HIGH') && f.status === 'OPEN') {
   831	        blocking.push({ reviewer: rec.reviewer, severity: f.severity, file: f.file || '(unspecified)', problem: f.problem });
   832	      }
   833	      // A FIXED finding must name a re-verifying record that is itself active
   834	      // and bound to this subject, and that reviewer may not be the builder.
   835	      if (f.status === 'FIXED') {
   836	        const v = active.find((r) => r.reviewId === f.verifiedByReviewId);
   837	        if (!v) {
   838	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-UNVERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED cites verifiedByReviewId "${f.verifiedByReviewId}", which is not an active review record for this subject.` });
   839	        } else if (v.reviewer === 'claude-self') {
   840	          problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-FIX-SELF-VERIFIED', detail: `${rec.reviewId}: finding marked FIXED is verified only by claude-self (${v.reviewId}). The builder cannot verify its own fix.` });
   841	        }
   842	      }
   843	    }
   844	  }
   845	  for (const b of blocking) {
   846	    problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-OPEN-BLOCKING-FINDING', detail: `${b.severity} from ${b.reviewer} in ${b.file}: ${b.problem}` });
   847	  }
   848	
   849	  // 11. Unverified items surfaced, never dropped.
   850	  const unverified = [];
   851	  for (const rec of active) for (const u of rec.unverified || []) unverified.push(`${rec.reviewer}: ${u}`);
   852	  if (typeof specRows !== 'undefined' && specRows) for (const r of specRows.unverified) unverified.push(`spec: ${r.source}`);
   853	
   854	  // 12. Deterministic checks. READY_FOR_PR is only reachable when this process
   855	  //     actually executed them — not when a caller asserts they passed.
   856	  let checks = null;
   857	  if (args.runChecks) {
   858	    if (!args.packet) {
   859	      problems.push({ rule: 'ENGOS-CHECKS-NO-PACKET', detail: '--run-checks needs a packet to read testsRequired from' });
   860	    } else {

   232	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   233	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   234	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   235	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   236	
   237	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   238	    switch (id) {
   239	      case 'spec':
   240	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   241	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   242	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   243	      case 'build':
   244	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   245	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   246	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   247	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   248	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   249	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   250	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   251	        }
   252	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   253	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   254	        }
   255	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   256	      case 'deterministic':
   257	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   258	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   260	      case 'codex':
   261	      case 'grok': {
   262	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   263	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   264	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   265	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   266	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   267	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   268	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   269	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   270	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   271	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   272	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   273	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   274	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   275	        }
   276	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   277	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   278	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   279	        }
   280	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   281	      }
   282	      case 'copilot':
   283	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   284	      case 'gate':
   285	        return parsed.ok
   286	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   287	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   288	      case 'runtime':
   289	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   290	      case 'checkpoint':
   291	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   292	      default:
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   294	    }
   295	  };
   296	
   297	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   298	}

   300	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   301	function projectEvents(limit) {
   302	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   303	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   304	  }
   305	  let l;
   306	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   307	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   308	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   309	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   310	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   311	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   312	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   313	    operation: e.operation || null,
   314	  }));
   315	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };
   316	}

   318	// ── snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   319	function snapshot(args) {
   320	  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();
   321	  const now = Date.parse(nowIso);
   322	  const engineering = projectEngineering(args);
   323	  const integration = { connectors: projectConnectors(now) };
   324	
   325	  // Failure isolation, made structural: these are two objects. There is no
   326	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   327	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   328	  return {
   329	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   330	    contract: {
   331	      absences: ['UNAVAILABLE', 'STALE', 'UNVERIFIED'],
   332	      note: 'Engineering and integration state are separate and are never blended. No field in this snapshot is synthesised; every value cites the artifact it came from.',
   333	    },
   334	    engineering,
   335	    integration,
   336	    // A THIRD plane, deliberately not merged into either of the others. It is
   337	    // not engineering (it holds no authority) and not integration (a Notion
   338	    // disagreement is not a connector fault). Separating it is precisely what
   339	    // makes "engineering VERIFIED / knowledge CONFLICT" a renderable pair
   340	    // rather than a contradiction something has to resolve.
   341	    knowledge: (() => {
   342	      try { return require('./knowledge-mirror.cjs').project({ now }); }
   343	      catch (e) {
   344	        // A refusal is a real, reportable state — never silently omitted.
   345	        return { state: 'REFUSED', reason: e.message, records: [], conflicts: 0 };
   346	      }
   347	    })(),
   348	    reviewers: projectReviewers(now),
   349	    events: projectEvents(Number(args.events) > 0 ? Number(args.events) : 12),
   350	  };
   351	}

   354	function parseArgs(argv) {
   355	  const a = {};
   356	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   357	    const t = argv[i];

   360	    else if (t === '--events') a.events = argv[++i];
   361	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   362	    else if (t === '--out') a.out = argv[++i];
   363	  }
   364	  return a;
   365	}

    80	function parseArgs(argv) {
    81	  const a = { port: 8791, host: '127.0.0.1' };
    82	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    83	    const t = argv[i];
    84	    if (t === '--port') a.port = Number(argv[++i]);
    85	    else if (t === '--host') a.host = argv[++i];
    86	    else if (t === '--token') a.token = argv[++i];
    87	    else if (t === '--allow-non-loopback') a.allowNonLoopback = true;
    88	    else if (t === '--i-understand-the-exposure') a.acknowledged = true;
    89	    else if (t === '--print-config') a.printConfig = true;
    90	  }
    91	  return a;
    92	}
    93	
    94	const LOOPBACK = new Set(['127.0.0.1', 'localhost', '::1']);
    95	
    96	/**
    97	 * Validate configuration. Returns {ok, reason, config}. Pure, so the refusal
    98	 * rules are testable without opening a socket.
    99	 */
   100	function validateConfig(args, env = process.env) {
   101	  if (!Number.isInteger(args.port) || args.port < 1 || args.port > 65535) {
   102	    return { ok: false, code: 'BAD_PORT', reason: `invalid port ${args.port}` };
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  const loopback = LOOPBACK.has(args.host);
   106	  if (!loopback) {
   107	    if (!args.allowNonLoopback) {
   108	      return {
   109	        ok: false, code: 'NON_LOOPBACK_REFUSED',
   110	        reason: `refusing to bind ${args.host}. This serves internal engineering state; binding beyond loopback requires --allow-non-loopback.`,
   111	      };
   112	    }
   113	    if (!args.acknowledged) {
   114	      return {
   115	        ok: false, code: 'EXPOSURE_UNACKNOWLEDGED',
   116	        reason: 'binding beyond loopback additionally requires --i-understand-the-exposure. Two flags, because a temporary open port is rarely temporary.',
   117	      };
   118	    }
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  // Auth is mandatory everywhere, including loopback.
   122	  let token = args.token || env.AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN || null;
   123	  let generated = false;
   124	  if (!token) {
   125	    if (!loopback) {
   126	      return {
   127	        ok: false, code: 'TOKEN_REQUIRED',
   128	        reason: 'a non-loopback bind requires an explicit --token or AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN. A generated token printed to a terminal is not an access-control plan for an exposed port.',
   129	      };
   130	    }
   131	    token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('base64url');
   132	    generated = true;
   133	  }
   134	  if (token.length < 24) {
   135	    return { ok: false, code: 'WEAK_TOKEN', reason: `token is ${token.length} characters; minimum 24.` };
   136	  }
   137	
   138	  return { ok: true, config: { ...args, host: args.host, token, generated, loopback } };
   139	}
   140	
   141	function isNeverServe(p) {
   142	  return NEVER_SERVE.some((re) => re.test(p));
   143	}
   144	
   145	function handler(config) {
   146	  return (req, res) => {
   147	    const started = Date.now();
   148	    const url = new URL(req.url, `http://${config.host}:${config.port}`);
   149	    const pathname = url.pathname;
   150	
   151	    const deny = (status, msg) => {
   152	      res.writeHead(status, {
   153	        'content-type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
   154	        'cache-control': 'no-store',
   155	        'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   156	        'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   157	      });
   158	      res.end(msg + '\n');
   159	      log(req, status, started);
   160	    };
   161	
   162	    if (req.method !== 'GET' && req.method !== 'HEAD') {
   163	      return deny(405, 'read-only host: only GET and HEAD are served');
   164	    }
   165	
   166	    // Auth first. Nothing below this line runs for an unauthenticated caller.
   167	    //
   168	    // THREE ACCEPTED CREDENTIALS, and the third is load-bearing:
   169	    // a token in the URL authenticates the DOCUMENT but not its SUBRESOURCES.
   170	    // The browser fetches state.js with no Authorization header and no query
   171	    // string, so a header-or-query-only design 401s the script tag and renders
   172	    // a blank dashboard — which is worse than an error, because the page looks
   173	    // like it loaded. On a valid token we set a derived, HttpOnly session
   174	    // cookie so subresources authenticate too. The cookie carries an HMAC of
   175	    // the token rather than the token itself, so the browser's cookie jar
   176	    // never holds the credential the operator actually typed.
   177	    const bearer = (req.headers.authorization || '').replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, '');
   178	    const queryToken = url.searchParams.get('token') || '';
   179	    const cookies = Object.fromEntries(
   180	      String(req.headers.cookie || '').split(';').map((c) => {
   181	        const i = c.indexOf('=');
   182	        return i === -1 ? [c.trim(), ''] : [c.slice(0, i).trim(), c.slice(i + 1).trim()];
   183	      }).filter(([k]) => k)
   184	    );
   185	    const sessionValue = sessionFor(config.token);
   186	    const primary = bearer || queryToken;
   187	    const authed =
   188	      (primary && timingSafeEqual(primary, config.token)) ||
   189	      (cookies.aegis_session && timingSafeEqual(cookies.aegis_session, sessionValue));
   190	
   191	    if (!authed) {
   192	      res.writeHead(401, {
   193	        'content-type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
   194	        'www-authenticate': 'Bearer realm="AEGIS"',
   195	        'cache-control': 'no-store',
   196	      });
   197	      res.end('unauthorized\n');
   198	      log(req, 401, started);
   199	      return;
   200	    }
   201	
   202	    // Establish the session cookie whenever a primary credential was used, so
   203	    // the document's subresources can authenticate on the next request.
   204	    const setCookie = (primary && timingSafeEqual(primary, config.token))
   205	      ? [`aegis_session=${sessionValue}; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=3600`]
   206	      : null;
   207	
   208	    if (isNeverServe(pathname)) return deny(403, 'refused: this class of path is never servable');
   209	
   210	    const entry = SERVABLE[pathname];
   211	    if (!entry) return deny(404, 'not found (only the dashboard projection is served here)');
   212	
   213	    // Resolve inside the dashboard directory and verify containment. The
   214	    // allow-list already prevents traversal; this catches a future mistake in
   215	    // the allow-list itself.
   216	    const file = path.resolve(DASHBOARD, entry.file);
   217	    if (!file.startsWith(DASHBOARD + path.sep)) return deny(403, 'refused: path escapes the dashboard directory');
   218	    if (!fs.existsSync(file)) {
   219	      return deny(503, `UNAVAILABLE: ${entry.file} has not been generated. Run:\n  node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js`);
   220	    }
   221	
   222	    const body = fs.readFileSync(file);
   223	    res.writeHead(200, {
   224	      ...(setCookie ? { 'set-cookie': setCookie } : {}),
   225	      'content-type': entry.type,
   226	      'content-length': body.length,
   227	      'cache-control': 'no-store',
   228	      'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   229	      'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   230	      'x-frame-options': 'DENY',
   231	      // The dashboard is self-contained: inline styles, no external anything.
   232	      'content-security-policy':
   233	        "default-src 'none'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data:; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'",
   234	    });
   235	    if (req.method === 'HEAD') res.end(); else res.end(body);
   236	    log(req, 200, started);
   237	  };
   238	}
   239	
   240	// Log the path and status, never the token or query string.
   241	function log(req, status, started) {
   242	  const p = String(req.url || '').split('?')[0];
   243	  process.stdout.write(`[aegis-host] ${status} ${req.method} ${p} ${Date.now() - started}ms\n`);
   244	}

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba builder-control/hosting/README.md | sed -n '1,82p'" in /Users/marcpapineau/.openclaw/worktrees/ai-engineering-os-v1
 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	# AEGIS Dashboard — Hosting
     2	
     3	A local, authenticated, read-only host for the AEGIS projection. It is a process
     4	you start and stop. It does not deploy, publish, tunnel, or register anything.
     5	
     6	## Run it
     7	
     8	```bash
     9	node builder-control/aegis-state.cjs --out builder-control/dashboard/state.js
    10	node builder-control/hosting/server.cjs
    11	```
    12	
    13	The first command regenerates the projection; the second serves it on
    14	`http://127.0.0.1:8791` and prints a one-run token. Open the printed URL.
    15	
    16	To use a stable token instead:
    17	
    18	```bash
    19	AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN="$(openssl rand -base64 32)" node builder-control/hosting/server.cjs
    20	```
    21	
    22	A supplied token is deliberately **never echoed** — this banner routinely lands
    23	in a log file or CI artifact, and echoing it would put a live credential at rest
    24	in all of them. A generated token is printed once and dies with the process.
    25	
    26	## What it serves — and nothing else
    27	
    28	`/` · `/index.html` · `/state.js`
    29	
    30	That is the complete allow-list. It is an allow-list rather than a filter
    31	because a filter is a list of the leaks somebody thought of. The repository,
    32	the ledger, task packets, review records, and raw reviewer transcripts are
    33	unreachable **by construction**. A second, independent refusal (`NEVER_SERVE`)
    34	blocks those path classes again in case the allow-list is ever widened by
    35	accident.
    36	
    37	Authentication does not widen this. An authenticated request for
    38	`/ledger.json` still gets `403`.
    39	
    40	## Refusals
    41	
    42	| configuration | result |
    43	|---|---|
    44	| loopback, no token supplied | allowed — a token is generated |
    45	| `--host 0.0.0.0` | `NON_LOOPBACK_REFUSED` |
    46	| `--host 0.0.0.0 --allow-non-loopback` | `EXPOSURE_UNACKNOWLEDGED` |
    47	| `--host 0.0.0.0` + both flags, no token | `TOKEN_REQUIRED` |
    48	| any token under 24 characters | `WEAK_TOKEN` |
    49	
    50	Two separate flags are required to leave loopback because a temporary open port
    51	is rarely temporary. There is **no anonymous mode**, including on loopback —
    52	any local process, browser tab, or package postinstall script can reach
    53	127.0.0.1, so loopback is not a boundary.
    54	
    55	## Headers
    56	
    57	`no-store` · `nosniff` · `X-Frame-Options: DENY` · CSP with
    58	`default-src 'none'` and `frame-ancestors 'none'`. The page is self-contained,
    59	so nothing external needs to load.
    60	
    61	## What this is not
    62	
    63	- **Not a deployment.** Nothing is published or exposed to the internet.
    64	- **Not durable.** Kill the process and it is gone.
    65	- **Not a workflow authority.** It serves a projection. Every value on the page
    66	  cites the artifact it came from, and absent data renders `UNAVAILABLE`,
    67	  `STALE`, or `UNVERIFIED` rather than a plausible number.
    68	
    69	## Before exposing this to anyone else
    70	
    71	The projection contains internal engineering process state — which reviewers
    72	ran, what blocked, which connectors are degraded. That is a map of where the
    73	soft spots are. It is not catastrophic if leaked and it does not belong on the
    74	open internet either. If it needs to reach another machine, prefer an SSH
    75	tunnel over binding a public interface:
    76	
    77	```bash
    78	ssh -N -L 8791:127.0.0.1:8791 <this-host>
    79	```
    80	
    81	That keeps the bind on loopback and puts the transport security somewhere that
    82	already has it.

   318	// ── snapshot ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   319	function snapshot(args) {
   320	  const nowIso = new Date().toISOString();
   321	  const now = Date.parse(nowIso);
   322	  const engineering = projectEngineering(args);
   323	  const integration = { connectors: projectConnectors(now) };
   324	
   325	  // Failure isolation, made structural: these are two objects. There is no
   326	  // combined "overall health" field, because the moment one exists somebody
   327	  // will let a failed connector darken a verified build.
   328	  return {
   329	    generatedAt: nowIso,
   330	    contract: {
   331	      absences: ['UNAVAILABLE', 'STALE', 'UNVERIFIED'],
   332	      note: 'Engineering and integration state are separate and are never blended. No field in this snapshot is synthesised; every value cites the artifact it came from.',
   333	    },
   334	    engineering,
   335	    integration,
   336	    // A THIRD plane, deliberately not merged into either of the others. It is
   337	    // not engineering (it holds no authority) and not integration (a Notion
   338	    // disagreement is not a connector fault). Separating it is precisely what
   339	    // makes "engineering VERIFIED / knowledge CONFLICT" a renderable pair
   340	    // rather than a contradiction something has to resolve.
   341	    knowledge: (() => {
   342	      try { return require('./knowledge-mirror.cjs').project({ now }); }
   343	      catch (e) {
   344	        // A refusal is a real, reportable state — never silently omitted.
   345	        return { state: 'REFUSED', reason: e.message, records: [], conflicts: 0 };
   346	      }
   347	    })(),
   348	    reviewers: projectReviewers(now),
   349	    events: projectEvents(Number(args.events) > 0 ? Number(args.events) : 12),
   350	  };
   351	}

   353	// ── CLI ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   354	function parseArgs(argv) {
   355	  const a = {};
   356	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
   357	    const t = argv[i];
   358	    if (t === '--subject-sha') a.subjectSha = argv[++i];
   359	    else if (t === '--packet') a.packet = argv[++i];
   360	    else if (t === '--events') a.events = argv[++i];
   361	    else if (t === '--json') a.json = true;
   362	    else if (t === '--out') a.out = argv[++i];
   363	  }
   364	  return a;
   365	}

   232	function deriveStages(parsed) {
   233	  const problems = parsed.problems || [];
   234	  const observed = (parsed.observed || []).join(' | ');
   235	  const has = (re) => problems.some((p) => re.test(p.rule + ' ' + p.detail));
   236	
   237	  const stageFor = (id) => {
   238	    switch (id) {
   239	      case 'spec':
   240	        if (has(/ENGOS-SPEC-UNPINNED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a source of truth is neither pinned nor marked UNVERIFIED' };
   241	        if (/spec pin:/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: observed.match(/spec pin: [^|]*/)[0].trim() };
   242	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no spec pin was evaluated' };
   243	      case 'build':
   244	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (codex REV-…-codex, CRITICAL/HIGH set, #8): this
   245	        // returned PASS unconditionally. A diff existing is not evidence that
   246	        // it builds — nothing in a gate verdict speaks to compilation at all.
   247	        // Rendering PASS here was the projector fabricating a positive state,
   248	        // which is precisely what the amendment forbids.
   249	        if (!parsed.subject || !(parsed.subject.subjectPaths || []).length) {
   250	          return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'no subject paths — there is nothing here to build' };
   251	        }
   252	        if (/deterministic checks executed here: (\d+)\/\1 passed/.test(observed)) {
   253	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate executed the declared checks and they passed' };
   254	        }
   255	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'a subject diff exists, but nothing in this verdict verifies it builds' };
   256	      case 'deterministic':
   257	        if (has(/ENGOS-DETERMINISTIC-FAILED/)) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'a declared check failed' };
   258	        if (/deterministic checks executed here/.test(observed)) return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'checks executed by the gate' };
   259	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'the gate did not run the checks (no --run-checks); they may have passed elsewhere, but this is not evidence of it' };
   260	      case 'codex':
   261	      case 'grok': {
   262	        const req = (parsed.classification && parsed.classification.requiredReviewers) || [];
   263	        if (!req.includes(id)) return { state: 'NOT_REQUIRED', reason: `not required for lane ${parsed.classification ? parsed.classification.lane : '?'}` };
   264	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEWER-UNAVAILABLE.*${id}|${id}.*UNAVAILABLE`, 'i'))) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'the reviewer could not run' };
   265	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-REJECTED.*${id}`, 'i'))) return { state: 'FAILED', reason: 'the reviewer returned REJECT' };
   266	        if (has(new RegExp(`ENGOS-REVIEW-MISSING.*\\b${id}\\b`, 'i'))) return { state: 'MISSING', reason: 'required, and no review record is bound to this subject' };
   267	        // CONFIRMED FINDING (#8): this used to fall through to PASS after
   268	        // checking only three failure shapes. Any rule the checks above did not
   269	        // recognise — a stale binding, short coverage, ambiguous records, a
   270	        // wrong packet — silently rendered as an approval. Absence of a known
   271	        // failure is not evidence of a pass. Require the gate to have positively
   272	        // observed this reviewer's disposition instead.
   273	        if (new RegExp(`(^|\\|)\\s*${id}: (APPROVE|APPROVE_WITH_NOTES)`, 'm').test(observed)) {
   274	          return { state: 'PASS', reason: 'the gate observed an approval bound to this subject' };
   275	        }
   276	        if (problems.some((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail))) {
   277	          const hit = problems.find((pr) => new RegExp(id, 'i').test(pr.rule + ' ' + pr.detail));
   278	          return { state: 'FAILED', reason: hit.rule + ': ' + String(hit.detail).slice(0, 120) };
   279	        }
   280	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no approval was observed for this reviewer on this subject' };
   281	      }
   282	      case 'copilot':
   283	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'advisory reviewer; entitlement unproven and no record collected' };
   284	      case 'gate':
   285	        return parsed.ok
   286	          ? { state: 'PASS', reason: parsed.state }
   287	          : { state: 'BLOCKED', reason: `${problems.length} unmet rule(s)` };
   288	      case 'runtime':
   289	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'runtime validation is a separate step and has not run' };
   290	      case 'checkpoint':
   291	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'no checkpoint is claimed by this gate verdict' };
   292	      default:
   293	        return { state: 'UNVERIFIED', reason: 'unmapped stage' };
   294	    }
   295	  };
   296	
   297	  return REQUIRED_STAGES.map((s) => ({ id: s.id, label: s.label, ...stageFor(s.id) }));
   298	}

   300	// ── recent events (the ledger IS the event bus — no second store) ───────────
   301	function projectEvents(limit) {
   302	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER)) {
   303	    return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger not found at ${rel(LEDGER)}`, events: [] };
   304	  }
   305	  let l;
   306	  try { l = readJSON(LEDGER); }
   307	  catch (e) { return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: `ledger unreadable: ${e.message}`, events: [] }; }
   308	  if (!Array.isArray(l)) return { state: 'UNAVAILABLE', reason: 'ledger is not an array', events: [] };
   309	  const events = l.slice(-limit).reverse().map((e) => ({
   310	    entryId: e.entryId, ts: e.ts, gate: e.gate, status: e.status,
   311	    agentId: e.agentId, packetId: e.packetId || null,
   312	    blockRule: e.blockRule || null,
   313	    operation: e.operation || null,
   314	  }));
   315	  return { state: 'OK', total: l.length, events, source: rel(LEDGER) };

   131	    // The disagreement that matters: the human store says done, AEGIS has no
   132	    // evidence. This is not resolved here and must not be.
   133	    const claimsDone = /^(DONE|COMPLETE|SHIPPED|VERIFIED)$/i.test(notion);
   134	    const conflict = claimsDone && aegis !== 'VERIFIED';
   135	    if (conflict) sync = 'CONFLICT';
   136	
   137	    rows.push({
   138	      recordId: r.recordId,
   139	      title: r.title || r.recordId,
   140	      notionStatus: notion,
   141	      aegisStatus: aegis,
   142	      syncState: sync,
   143	      lastSyncedAt: r.lastSyncedAt || null,
   144	      ageMinutes: r.lastSyncedAt ? ageMinutes(r.lastSyncedAt, now) : null,
   145	      conflict: conflict
   146	        ? {
   147	            kind: 'SOURCE-OF-TRUTH CONFLICT',
   148	            notion, aegis,
   149	            requiredAction: 'RECONCILE — Product Owner decision required. This is not resolved automatically in either direction.',
   150	          }
   151	        : null,
   152	      // Human proposals are inert until they enter the workflow.
   153	      proposal: r.proposedChange
   154	        ? { state: 'PROPOSED', detail: r.proposedChange,
   155	            note: 'A Product Owner change becomes a PROPOSED objective and enters the normal loop. It never modifies code directly and never skips a stage.' }
   156	        : null,
   157	    });
   158	  }
   159	  return rows;
   160	}
   161	
   162	function project(opts = {}) {
   163	  const now = typeof opts.now === 'number' ? opts.now : Date.now();
   164	  const m = readMirror(opts.mirror);
   165	  if (m.state === 'DISCONNECTED' || m.state === 'FAILED') {
   166	    return {
   167	      state: m.state, reason: m.reason, source: m.source,
   168	      records: [], conflicts: 0,
   169	      authorityNote: 'The knowledge mirror holds human memory and never engineering authority.',
   170	    };
   171	  }
   172	  assertNoSelfCertification(m.records);
   173	  const rows = reconcile(m.records, opts.aegisVerifiedById || {}, now, m.raw.staleAfterMinutes);
   174	  return {
   175	    state: 'OK',
   176	    source: m.source,
   177	    records: rows,
   178	    conflicts: rows.filter((r) => r.conflict).length,
   179	    syncStates: SYNC_STATES,
   180	    authorityNote: 'The knowledge mirror holds human memory and never engineering authority.',
   181	  };
   182	}

    80	function parseArgs(argv) {
    81	  const a = { port: 8791, host: '127.0.0.1' };
    82	  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {
    83	    const t = argv[i];
    84	    if (t === '--port') a.port = Number(argv[++i]);
    85	    else if (t === '--host') a.host = argv[++i];
    86	    else if (t === '--token') a.token = argv[++i];
    87	    else if (t === '--allow-non-loopback') a.allowNonLoopback = true;
    88	    else if (t === '--i-understand-the-exposure') a.acknowledged = true;
    89	    else if (t === '--print-config') a.printConfig = true;
    90	  }
    91	  return a;
    92	}
    93	
    94	const LOOPBACK = new Set(['127.0.0.1', 'localhost', '::1']);
    95	
    96	/**
    97	 * Validate configuration. Returns {ok, reason, config}. Pure, so the refusal
    98	 * rules are testable without opening a socket.
    99	 */
   100	function validateConfig(args, env = process.env) {
   101	  if (!Number.isInteger(args.port) || args.port < 1 || args.port > 65535) {
   102	    return { ok: false, code: 'BAD_PORT', reason: `invalid port ${args.port}` };
   103	  }
   104	
   105	  const loopback = LOOPBACK.has(args.host);
   106	  if (!loopback) {
   107	    if (!args.allowNonLoopback) {
   108	      return {
   109	        ok: false, code: 'NON_LOOPBACK_REFUSED',
   110	        reason: `refusing to bind ${args.host}. This serves internal engineering state; binding beyond loopback requires --allow-non-loopback.`,
   111	      };
   112	    }
   113	    if (!args.acknowledged) {
   114	      return {
   115	        ok: false, code: 'EXPOSURE_UNACKNOWLEDGED',
   116	        reason: 'binding beyond loopback additionally requires --i-understand-the-exposure. Two flags, because a temporary open port is rarely temporary.',
   117	      };
   118	    }
   119	  }
   120	
   121	  // Auth is mandatory everywhere, including loopback.
   122	  let token = args.token || env.AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN || null;
   123	  let generated = false;
   124	  if (!token) {
   125	    if (!loopback) {
   126	      return {
   127	        ok: false, code: 'TOKEN_REQUIRED',
   128	        reason: 'a non-loopback bind requires an explicit --token or AEGIS_DASHBOARD_TOKEN. A generated token printed to a terminal is not an access-control plan for an exposed port.',
   129	      };
   130	    }
   131	    token = crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('base64url');
   132	    generated = true;
   133	  }
   134	  if (token.length < 24) {
   135	    return { ok: false, code: 'WEAK_TOKEN', reason: `token is ${token.length} characters; minimum 24.` };
   136	  }
   137	
   138	  return { ok: true, config: { ...args, host: args.host, token, generated, loopback } };
   139	}
   140	
   141	function isNeverServe(p) {
   142	  return NEVER_SERVE.some((re) => re.test(p));
   143	}

   145	function handler(config) {
   146	  return (req, res) => {
   147	    const started = Date.now();
   148	    const url = new URL(req.url, `http://${config.host}:${config.port}`);
   149	    const pathname = url.pathname;
   150	
   151	    const deny = (status, msg) => {
   152	      res.writeHead(status, {
   153	        'content-type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
   154	        'cache-control': 'no-store',
   155	        'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   156	        'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   157	      });
   158	      res.end(msg + '\n');
   159	      log(req, status, started);
   160	    };
   161	
   162	    if (req.method !== 'GET' && req.method !== 'HEAD') {
   163	      return deny(405, 'read-only host: only GET and HEAD are served');
   164	    }
   165	
   166	    // Auth first. Nothing below this line runs for an unauthenticated caller.
   167	    //
   168	    // THREE ACCEPTED CREDENTIALS, and the third is load-bearing:
   169	    // a token in the URL authenticates the DOCUMENT but not its SUBRESOURCES.
   170	    // The browser fetches state.js with no Authorization header and no query
   171	    // string, so a header-or-query-only design 401s the script tag and renders
   172	    // a blank dashboard — which is worse than an error, because the page looks
   173	    // like it loaded. On a valid token we set a derived, HttpOnly session
   174	    // cookie so subresources authenticate too. The cookie carries an HMAC of
   175	    // the token rather than the token itself, so the browser's cookie jar

 succeeded in 0ms:
   117	// ─── Read the current ledger array (creates empty array if file absent) ────────
   118	function readLedger() {
   119	  if (!fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) return [];
   120	  const raw = fs.readFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, 'utf8').trim();
   121	  if (!raw) return [];
   122	  const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
   123	  if (!Array.isArray(parsed)) {
   124	    throw new Error(`Ledger at ${LEDGER_FILE} is not a JSON array — aborting to prevent data loss`);
   125	  }
   126	  return parsed;
   127	}
   128	
   129	// ─── Write the ledger, asserting old entries are byte-identical ───────────────
   130	function writeLedger(oldEntries, newEntry) {
   131	  const newArray  = [...oldEntries, newEntry];
   132	
   133	  // Safety: re-read and verify old entries are unchanged
   134	  if (fs.existsSync(LEDGER_FILE)) {
   135	    const onDisk = readLedger();
   136	    if (onDisk.length < oldEntries.length) {
   137	      throw new Error('LEDGER INTEGRITY VIOLATION: on-disk array is shorter than expected — refusing write');
   138	    }
   139	    // Byte-compare each old entry
   140	    for (let i = 0; i < oldEntries.length; i++) {
   141	      if (JSON.stringify(onDisk[i]) !== JSON.stringify(oldEntries[i])) {
   142	        throw new Error(`LEDGER INTEGRITY VIOLATION: entry at index ${i} differs on disk vs in memory — another process may have written concurrently`);
   143	      }
   144	    }
   145	  }
   146	
   147	  fs.writeFileSync(LEDGER_FILE, JSON.stringify(newArray, null, 2) + '\n', 'utf8');
   148	  return newArray.length;
   149	}
   150	
   151	// ─── Validate an entry object against the ledger-entry schema ─────────────────
   152	function validateEntry(entry) {
   153	  const schema = loadJSON(SCHEMA_FILE);
   154	  const defs   = schema['$defs'] || {};
   155	  return validateSchema(schema, entry, '#', defs);
   156	}
   157	
   158	// ─── --append <entry.json> ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   159	function appendEntry(entryPath) {
   160	  // Load entry
   161	  let entry;
   162	  try {
   163	    entry = loadJSON(path.resolve(entryPath));
   164	  } catch (e) {
   165	    console.error(`ERROR loading entry file: ${e.message}`);
   166	    process.exit(2);
   167	  }
   168	
   169	  // Validate
   170	  const errors = validateEntry(entry);
   171	  if (errors.length) {
   172	    console.error('SCHEMA VALIDATION FAILED for entry:');
   173	    errors.forEach(e => console.error(`  • ${e}`));
   174	    process.exit(1);
   175	  }
   176	
   177	  // Append
   178	  const before = readLedger();
   179	  const newLen = writeLedger(before, entry);
   180	
   181	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Appended entry "${entry.entryId}" to ${LEDGER_FILE}`);
   182	  console.log(`[ledger-writer] Ledger length: ${before.length} → ${newLen}  (grew by 1)`);
   183	  process.exit(0);
   184	}

   156	  };
   157	  const ratio = (a, b) => { const l1 = lum(a), l2 = lum(b); const hi = Math.max(l1, l2), lo = Math.min(l1, l2); return (hi + 0.05) / (lo + 0.05); };
   158	  const bg = vars['--bg-1'];

   161	    assert.ok(vars[t], `token ${t} missing`);
   162	    const r = ratio(vars[t], bg);
   163	    assert.ok(r >= 3, `${t} (${vars[t]}) is ${r.toFixed(2)}:1 on ${bg} — below the 3:1 this design requires`);
   164	  }

   102	function ageMinutes(iso, now) {
   103	  const t = Date.parse(iso);
   104	  return Number.isNaN(t) ? null : Math.floor((now - t) / 60000);
   105	}
   106	
   107	/**
   108	 * Compare what the human store believes against what AEGIS actually verified.
   109	 * `aegisVerified` is supplied by the caller from real gate output — this module
   110	 * never infers it.
   111	 */
   112	function reconcile(records, aegisVerifiedById, now, staleAfterMin = 1440) {
   113	  const rows = [];
   114	  for (const r of records) {
   115	    const notion = r.notionStatus || 'UNKNOWN';
   116	    const aegis = aegisVerifiedById && Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty.call(aegisVerifiedById, r.recordId)
   117	      ? aegisVerifiedById[r.recordId]
   118	      : 'NOT_VERIFIED';
   119	
   120	    let sync = SYNC_STATES.includes(r.syncState) ? r.syncState : 'DISCONNECTED';
   121	
   122	    // A write that was never confirmed is not a write.
   123	    if (sync === 'SYNCED' && !r.lastSyncedAt) {
   124	      sync = 'PENDING';
   125	    } else if (sync === 'SYNCED' && r.lastSyncedAt) {
   126	      const age = ageMinutes(r.lastSyncedAt, now);
   127	      if (age === null) sync = 'PENDING';
   128	      else if (age > staleAfterMin) sync = 'STALE';
   129	    }
   130	
   131	    // The disagreement that matters: the human store says done, AEGIS has no
   132	    // evidence. This is not resolved here and must not be.
   133	    const claimsDone = /^(DONE|COMPLETE|SHIPPED|VERIFIED)$/i.test(notion);
   134	    const conflict = claimsDone && aegis !== 'VERIFIED';
   135	    if (conflict) sync = 'CONFLICT';
   136	
   137	    rows.push({
   138	      recordId: r.recordId,
   139	      title: r.title || r.recordId,
   140	      notionStatus: notion,
   141	      aegisStatus: aegis,
   142	      syncState: sync,
   143	      lastSyncedAt: r.lastSyncedAt || null,
   144	      ageMinutes: r.lastSyncedAt ? ageMinutes(r.lastSyncedAt, now) : null,
   145	      conflict: conflict
   146	        ? {
   147	            kind: 'SOURCE-OF-TRUTH CONFLICT',
   148	            notion, aegis,
   149	            requiredAction: 'RECONCILE — Product Owner decision required. This is not resolved automatically in either direction.',
   150	          }
   151	        : null,
   152	      // Human proposals are inert until they enter the workflow.
   153	      proposal: r.proposedChange
   154	        ? { state: 'PROPOSED', detail: r.proposedChange,
   155	            note: 'A Product Owner change becomes a PROPOSED objective and enters the normal loop. It never modifies code directly and never skips a stage.' }
   156	        : null,
   157	    });
   158	  }
   159	  return rows;
   160	}
   161	
   162	function project(opts = {}) {
   163	  const now = typeof opts.now === 'number' ? opts.now : Date.now();
   164	  const m = readMirror(opts.mirror);
   165	  if (m.state === 'DISCONNECTED' || m.state === 'FAILED') {
   166	    return {
   167	      state: m.state, reason: m.reason, source: m.source,
   168	      records: [], conflicts: 0,
   169	      authorityNote: 'The knowledge mirror holds human memory and never engineering authority.',
   170	    };
   171	  }
   172	  assertNoSelfCertification(m.records);
   173	  const rows = reconcile(m.records, opts.aegisVerifiedById || {}, now, m.raw.staleAfterMinutes);
   174	  return {
   175	    state: 'OK',
   176	    source: m.source,
   177	    records: rows,
   178	    conflicts: rows.filter((r) => r.conflict).length,
   179	    syncStates: SYNC_STATES,
   180	    authorityNote: 'The knowledge mirror holds human memory and never engineering authority.',
   181	  };
   182	}

   189	serious flaw that previous engineers missed, and try to prove it incorrect.
   190	
   191	Attack: requirement interpretation, edge cases, regression paths, authorization,
   192	security, data integrity, state, concurrency, error paths, dependency
   193	assumptions, and the tests themselves. Specifically hunt for cases where the
   194	tests PASS but a user still experiences wrong behaviour.
   195

   197

   199	${objective}
   200	
   201	SUBJECT PATHS (${subject.subjectPaths.length}):
   202	${subject.subjectPaths.map((p) => '  ' + p).join('\n')}
   203	
   204	DIFF UNDER REVIEW:
   205	${diff}
   206	${RECORD_CONTRACT}`;
   207	}

   209	// ── raw output -> record ────────────────────────────────────────────────────
   210	// Extract the first balanced JSON object. Models wrap JSON in prose and fences
   211	// no matter how firmly the contract says not to; that is a formatting quirk,
   212	// not a reason to discard a real review.
   213	function extractJson(text) {
   214	  if (typeof text !== 'string') return null;
   215	  const start = text.indexOf('{');
   216	  if (start === -1) return null;
   217	  let depth = 0, inStr = false, esc = false;
   218	  for (let i = start; i < text.length; i++) {
   219	    const c = text[i];
   220	    if (inStr) {
   221	      if (esc) esc = false;
   222	      else if (c === '\\') esc = true;
   223	      else if (c === '"') inStr = false;
   224	      continue;
   225	    }
   226	    if (c === '"') inStr = true;
   227	    else if (c === '{') depth++;
   228	    else if (c === '}') {
   229	      depth--;
   230	      if (depth === 0) {
   231	        try { return JSON.parse(text.slice(start, i + 1)); } catch { return null; }
   232	      }
   233	    }
   234	  }
   235	  return null;

   145	function handler(config) {
   146	  return (req, res) => {
   147	    const started = Date.now();
   148	    const url = new URL(req.url, `http://${config.host}:${config.port}`);
   149	    const pathname = url.pathname;
   150	
   151	    const deny = (status, msg) => {
   152	      res.writeHead(status, {
   153	        'content-type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8',
   154	        'cache-control': 'no-store',
   155	        'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff',
   156	        'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer',
   157	      });
   158	      res.end(msg + '\n');
   159	      log(req, status, started);
   160	    };
   161	
   162	    if (req.method !== 'GET' && req.method !== 'HEAD') {
   163	      return deny(405, 'read-only host: only GET and HEAD are served');
   164	    }
   165	
   166	    // Auth first. Nothing below this line runs for an unauthenticated caller.
   167	    //
   168	    // THREE ACCEPTED CREDENTIALS, and the third is load-bearing:
   169	    // a token in the URL authenticates the DOCUMENT but not its SUBRESOURCES.
   170	    // The browser fetches state.js with no Authorization header and no query
   171	    // string, so a header-or-query-only design 401s the script tag and renders
   172	    // a blank dashboard — which is worse than an error, because the page looks
   173	    // like it loaded. On a valid token we set a derived, HttpOnly session
   174	    // cookie so subresources authenticate too. The cookie carries an HMAC of
   175	    // the token rather than the token itself, so the browser's cookie jar
   176	    // never holds the credential the operator actually typed.
   177	    const bearer = (req.headers.authorization || '').replace(/^Bearer\s+/i, '');
   178	    const queryToken = url.searchParams.get('token') || '';
   179	    const cookies = Object.fromEntries(

   873	// Execute the packet's declared testsRequired. These are command strings from a
   874	// packet that has already passed packet-tools validation and the Builder
   875	// Control gate; they are run through `bash -lc` because that is what they are —
   876	// commands. No caller-supplied data is interpolated into them.
   877	function runDeterministicChecks(packetPath) {
   878	  let pkt;
   879	  try { pkt = readJSON(packetPath); } catch { return []; }
   880	  const cmds = (pkt.testsRequired || []).filter((c) => !/--gate-done/.test(c));
   881	  const results = [];
   882	  for (const cmd of cmds) {
   883	    const r = spawnSync('bash', ['-lc', cmd], { cwd: ROOT, encoding: 'utf8', maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 * 1024 });
   884	    results.push({ cmd, exit: r.status === null ? 1 : r.status, tail: ((r.stdout || '') + (r.stderr || '')).trim().split('\n').slice(-3).join('\n') });
   885	  }
   886	  return results;

   478	// A missing protected-path policy must be a hard block, never an empty

   480	(function missingPolicyFailsClosed() {
   481	  const live = path.join(ROOT, 'builder-control', 'protected-paths.json');

   483	  if (!fs.existsSync(live)) { fail++; console.error('  FAIL protected-paths.json is missing before the test even ran'); return; }

   486	  let r;
   487	  try {
   488	    fs.renameSync(live, hidden);
   489	    r = run(['--classify', '--changed', 'src/app.ts']);
   490	  } finally {
   491	    restore();
   492	  }
   493	  const ok = r && r.exit === 3 && /ENGOS-POLICY-UNAVAILABLE/.test(r.out) && /an absent policy is not an empty policy/.test(r.out);
   494	  if (ok) {
   495	    pass++; console.log('  ok   a missing protected-path policy HARD-BLOCKS  (exit 3)');

   500	  }

    80	---
    81	
    82	## 3. What counts as high-risk
    83	
    84	Path-shaped, so it is knowable before anything is read and cannot be argued
    85	with: auth/session/SSO · secrets, tokens, credentials · payments · database
    86	migrations and schema · permissions, roles, tenant isolation · cryptography ·
    87	security controls (CSRF, CORS, sanitisation) · `.github/workflows/**` ·
    88	`builder-control/**` · infrastructure definitions · anything on
    89	`protected-paths.json`.
    90	
    91	Plus two you declare: `--milestone` and `--novel`.
    92	
    93	High-risk adds the red team and nothing else changes.
    94	
    95	---
    96	
    97	## 4. The order, and why it is that order
    98	
    99	```
   100	requirement + spec pin
   101	   ↓                        cheap, and everything downstream is meaningless without it
   102	baseline recorded
   103	   ↓                        so pre-existing failures are never blamed on this change
   104	Claude builds
   105	   ↓
   106	deterministic checks        build · typecheck · lint · tests · gate.sh
   107	   ↓                        ── free. Run before spending a model on review.
   108	Codex reviews independently
   109	   ↓                        given the requirement, spec, diff, tests — NOT Claude's reasoning
   110	corrections (max 3 cycles)
   111	   ↓                        then STOP and escalate; models will optimise against
   112	   ↓                        each other forever if allowed to
   113	PR opened
   114	   ↓
   115	GitHub CI + Copilot guardian
   116	   ↓                        CI blocks. Copilot advises — and can block on CRITICAL/HIGH.
   117	Grok red team               HIGH-RISK / novel / security / milestone ONLY. Read-only.
   118	   ↓
   119	runtime validation          the actual app, the actual request, the actual page
   120	   ↓

   752	  // 6. Supersession — the one documented deterministic replacement rule.
   753	  //    A record may declare `supersedes: <reviewId>`; the named record is
   754	  //    dropped. Anything else that leaves two records for the same reviewer on
   755	  //    the same subject is AMBIGUOUS and blocks, because picking a winner by
   756	  //    timestamp or file order would let whichever record happened to sort
   757	  //    later silently overrule a rejection.
   758	  const superseded = new Set();
   759	  for (const rec of bound) if (rec.supersedes) superseded.add(rec.supersedes);
   760	  const active = bound.filter((r) => !superseded.has(r.reviewId));
   761	  if (superseded.size) observed.push(`${superseded.size} record(s) superseded by an explicit supersedes declaration`);
   762

   770	      });
   771	    }
   772	  }

   628	function loadReview(p) {
   629	  if (!fs.existsSync(p)) return { path: p, ok: false, errors: [`file not found: ${p}`] };
   630	  let rec;
   631	  try { rec = readJSON(p); } catch (e) { return { path: p, ok: false, errors: [`not valid JSON: ${e.message}`] }; }
   632	  const schema = readJSON(REVIEW_SCHEMA);
   633	  const errors = [...validateAgainst(schema, rec), ...reviewSemantics(rec)];
   634	  return { path: p, ok: errors.length === 0, errors, rec };
   635	}

   637	function cmdValidateReview(args) {
   638	  const files = args.review.length ? args.review : args._;
   639	  if (!files.length) die('usage: --validate-review <review.json> [more.json ...]', EXIT_USAGE);
   640	  let bad = 0;
   641	  const results = files.map(loadReview);
   642	  if (args.json) {
   643	    out(JSON.stringify(results.map((r) => ({ path: r.path, ok: r.ok, errors: r.errors })), null, 2));
   644	    return results.every((r) => r.ok) ? EXIT_PASS : EXIT_USAGE;
   645	  }
   646	  out('ENGINEERING OS — REVIEW RECORD VALIDATION');
   647	  out('='.repeat(52));
   648	  for (const r of results) {
   649	    if (r.ok) { out(`ok    ${r.path}  (${r.rec.reviewer} / ${r.rec.disposition})`); }
   650	    else { bad++; out(`FAIL  ${r.path}`); for (const e of r.errors) out(`        ${e}`); }
   651	  }
   652	  out('');
   653	  out(bad ? `${bad} invalid record(s).` : `All ${results.length} record(s) valid.`);
   654	  return bad ? EXIT_USAGE : EXIT_PASS;
   655	}

   659	function collectReviewPaths(args) {

   662	  const fromDir = fs.existsSync(dir)
   663	    ? fs.readdirSync(dir).filter((f) => f.endsWith('.json')).map((f) => path.join(dir, f))
   664	    : [];
   665	  return [...new Set([...explicit, ...fromDir])];
   666	}
